        Idea:
# brief-unify-capacity-control-20260513

**Profile:** `all-codex/standard/high`
**Repo:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator` (Python, Railway-deployed) + small cross-repo touch in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py`
**Branch base:** `origin/main` at `fcec14f`
**Rollout:** shadow-mode → divergence audit → flag-flip cutover

> **Brief revision history:** drafted 2026-05-13. Sense-checked by Codex GPT-5.5 `high` (read-only, repo-aware). Corrections from that review folded in below — most importantly: code-level claims about `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` and the cycle phase order have been corrected against ground truth.

---

## Goal

Replace the orchestrator's two-controller scaling loop with one durable-intent reconciler. Specifically:

1. Introduce `worker_capacity_intents` (Postgres) as the durable source of truth for "what capacity should exist right now, for what reason, with what cooldowns."
2. Build `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py` with an explicit five-phase state machine: **OBSERVE → PLAN → ADMIT → ACT → RECORD**.
3. Delete `_handle_early_termination` entirely. Its job becomes a `cancel_pending_spawn` action emitted by the reconciler under strictly-defined conditions.
4. Fold RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff into the same reconciler as admission control (no separate guardrail path).
5. Delete the test-only duplicate code path under `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` (which is *not* the production runtime path — see "Context" below).
6. Roll out via shadow mode: new reconciler runs alongside the existing controller, logs intended actions, doesn't take effect until a feature flag flips.

Success is measured by **production effective-utilisation rising from ~4% to ≥70%** sustained over 24h post-cutover, with no user-task failures attributable to the new controller.

---

## Context — what's broken

A 2-hour rollout window on 2026-05-13 observed **73 GPU workers spawned, 70 killed before becoming productive, 1 worker did all the actual work** (4% effective utilisation). The same pattern is present in git history going back at least 6 weeks.

Two parallel Codex GPT-5.5 sense-checks (independent runs, no cross-talk) reading `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` converged on a single architectural diagnosis, **subsequently verified by a third Codex `high` review** against the actual code:

> The orchestrator runs **two independent capacity controllers** in one cycle. `_handle_early_termination` (around `control_loop.py:340`, `current_capacity` derived at `:362`, gate check at `:377`) computes one `desired` figure from `task_counts.total * scale_down_multiplier` + min GPUs + idle buffer. Later in the same cycle, `_calculate_scaling_decision` calls `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` at `control_loop.py:802` (when `use_new_scaling_logic` is enabled), which emits spawn action math from queued coverage (`workers_covering_tasks = idle + spawning`, `uncovered_tasks = queued - workers_covering_tasks`, `spawn_for_tasks = uncovered_tasks`, see `worker_state.py:588`) plus a busy-worker buffer floor (`worker_state.py:594`). **However**, `desired_workers` itself is still populated from the legacy `total_workload * scale_up_multiplier`, `busy + idle buffer`, and `min_active_gpus` block at `worker_state.py:616`.
>
> The bug is **not** "stale snapshot between phases" — the scaling decision is computed *after* early-term, not before. The bug is that early-term **mutates the worker list** (terminating spawning workers) before a second, separate scaling formula runs against the same phase-1 task snapshot and the now-changed worker list. Two formulas, two notions of `desired`, one cycle, opposing actions.

Three named failure modes ride on this defect:

- **Spawn churn from snapshot gates.** `_handle_early_termination` is guarded by `if task_counts.queued > 0` (at `control_loop.py:377`). `task_counts.queued` is an instantaneous snapshot taken once at the top of each cycle. When the single productive worker had just claimed a segment (queue momentarily empty, six in-progress segments active), early-term fired and killed the spawning replacement. Next cycle re-spawned. Loop.
- **No backoff for RunPod `spawn_failed`.** 55 of the 73 deaths were RunPod refusing pod allocation. The orchestrator retried at 30-second cycle cadence indefinitely.
- **Mutating actions sequenced before mismatched re-derivation.** Early-term terminates spawning workers from the worker list, then `_calculate_scaling_decision` re-derives `desired` against the same phase-1 task snapshot but a now-shorter worker list, and decides to re-spawn what early-term just killed.

A team-authored postmortem (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-postmortem-20260513.md`) and approach memo (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-approach-20260513.md`) cover the symptom-level analysis. The architectural prescription below is the *deep* fix.

### Duplicate code path — clarified

`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` is **the** production runtime path. `main.py` imports `OrchestratorControlLoop` from it. The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree contains duplicated chunks of capacity logic (including a duplicate `_handle_early_termination`) but is **only exercised by tests, not by production**. Default deletion target for this sprint: the mixin duplication under `control/phases/`. The planner may override that choice only by first showing concretely that the mixins contain logic worth migrating into `control_loop.py` (or by proposing to flip production over to the mixin path — but that is out of scope for this sprint and would be a separate brief).

---

## Architecture — what we're building

### New Postgres table

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.worker_capacity_intents (
  id                          uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  pool                        text NOT NULL,
  route_key                   text,                              -- nullable for pool-wide intents
  desired_capacity            int  NOT NULL,
  reason                      text NOT NULL,                     -- 'queued_coverage'|'min_floor'|'cooldown'|...
  observed_queued             int,
  observed_active             int,
  observed_spawning           int,
  cycle_id                    bigint,
  created_at                  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  valid_until                 timestamptz,                       -- intent expires; absence ≠ kill
  stable_since                timestamptz,                       -- when desired_capacity last changed
  consecutive_spawn_failures  int  NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  next_spawn_allowed_at       timestamptz,                       -- backoff cooldown
  shadow                      boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true      -- shadow vs authoritative mode
);

CREATE INDEX worker_capacity_intents_pool_route_created
  ON public.worker_capacity_intents (pool, route_key, created_at DESC);
```

**One row written per cycle per pool**, regardless of whether the intent changed. This is the audit trail.

**Migration location:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/` (alongside the existing `2025*_*.sql` files). Filename pattern: `20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`. The repo's existing migration registration system applies — planner should locate how it's wired (likely `scripts/` or a runner referenced from Railway config) and follow the established pattern.

### Logging — `system_logs` constraint

Existing `system_logs.valid_source_type` CHECK constraint permits only `orchestrator_gpu`, `orchestrator_api`, and `worker`. **Do not invent a new `source_type` value.** Use `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` with a metadata tag field (e.g., `metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' = 'true'`) to distinguish shadow log entries from authoritative ones. Saves a migration and avoids breaking other readers of `system_logs`.

### New Python module — `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`

Owns the full five-phase state machine. Pure-where-possible, side-effects concentrated at boundaries.

```python
class CapacityReconciler:
    def reconcile(self, cycle_id: int) -> ReconciliationResult:
        observed = self.observe()                     # phase 1: read workers + tasks
        plan = self.plan(observed)                    # phase 2: compute CapacityPlan
        admitted = self.admit(plan)                   # phase 3: filter by backoff/cooldown
        result = self.act(admitted, shadow=self.shadow_mode)  # phase 4: side effects (or log-only)
        self.record(cycle_id, observed, plan, admitted, result)  # phase 5: persist intent row
        return result
```

Where:

- **`observe()`** is the canonical read of `workers` + `tasks` state. Counts active / spawning / route-stale / idle, computes `total_tasks = queued + in_progress`. Returns a frozen `Observed` dataclass — **immutable**, taken at one moment in time, passed by value through subsequent phases.
- **`plan(observed)`** computes ONE `desired_capacity` from ONE formula. Returns a `CapacityPlan` with `desired`, `effective_capacity = active + spawning - admitted_pending_cancels`, `actions = [Spawn | CancelPendingSpawn | TerminateIdle | Noop]`, `reason`, `valid_until`.
- **`admit(plan)`** is admission control. Checks `next_spawn_allowed_at` per pool, drops `Spawn` actions during cooldown, logs `suppressed_by_backoff`.
- **`act(admitted, shadow)`** executes side effects when `shadow=False`. When `shadow=True`, logs intended actions to `system_logs` (source_type=`orchestrator_gpu`, metadata tag `shadow_intended_action`). **In shadow mode, `act()` MUST NOT call RunPod, MUST NOT mutate worker rows, MUST NOT update backoff state. Shadow is read-only on the external world.**
- **`record(...)`** writes one row to `worker_capacity_intents` regardless of mode. The `shadow` boolean column distinguishes shadow rows from authoritative rows.

### Rules the reconciler MUST honor (invariants)

These are invariants, not preferences. The cycle-trace simulation tests in the deliverables section exist to enforce them.

1. **Spawning workers count as capacity** until they (a) report `spawn_failed`, (b) exceed `active_initializing_max_sec`, or (c) are explicitly cancelled by a `CancelPendingSpawn` action emitted by this reconciler.
2. **Never kill or cancel a spawning worker because `queued == 0`.** The queue snapshot does not have authority over an authorized spawn.
3. **Cancel `Spawn` action emission** only when the desired_capacity has remained ≤ effective for **at least two consecutive observed reconciler snapshots spanning at least `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time**, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Wall-clock based, not iteration-count based — duplicate Railway replicas or delayed cycles make raw cycle count unreliable.
4. **Scale down active workers** only via idle timeout + min/buffer constraints. Active workers are never killed by capacity-pressure logic.
5. **RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff is keyed by `(pool, route_key)`**, not globally. `consecutive_spawn_failures += 1` and `next_spawn_allowed_at = now() + min(2^N * 30s, 600s)`. Successful spawn for that `(pool, route_key)` resets the counter to 0.
6. **`record()` writes exactly one row per pool per cycle**, even when nothing changed. Audit trail integrity.
7. **No authoritative spawn action executes without a prior `RECORD` write succeeding** within the same reconciliation. This means `act(shadow=False)` calls `record()` first and only then dispatches RunPod creates / cancellations. Idempotency: if `act()` partially completes and the process crashes, the next cycle sees the recorded intent and can resume.
8. **Single-writer per pool per cycle in authoritative mode.** Use a Postgres advisory lock keyed on `hashtext(pool)` or a lease row in a small `orchestrator_leases` table, held for the duration of `admit()` + `act()`. Without this, two Railway replicas can each spawn against the same pool's intent and double-provision. (Shadow mode does not need the lock — multiple shadows are fine since they don't mutate.)
9. **At cutover, the reconciler must adopt pre-existing spawning workers** that have no associated intent row. These were spawned by the legacy controller before cutover. Treat them as part of effective_capacity; assign a synthetic adoption intent on first observe, do not cancel them.

### Phasing — what gets deleted, what survives

**Deleted:**
- `_handle_early_termination` (the entire phase 2 block in `control_loop.py` at lines ~340-400).
- The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree (test-only duplicate code path). Includes any tests that exercised it; replace those with tests against the new reconciler.

**Survives:**
- Worker lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers`, heartbeat checks, promote-to-active logic) — stays in `control_loop.py`.
- RunPod pod creation primitives (`runpod_client.create_pod` and friends) — stay. `act()` calls into them.
- `min_active_gpus`, `machines_to_keep_idle`, `buffer_based` calculations — stay as **inputs** to `plan()`. **Out of scope for this sprint** is simplifying or renaming them; `plan()` consumes them and emits one authoritative `desired` from one formula. The planner MUST write down exactly that composition (what's the precedence order between `min_active_gpus` floor, `queued_coverage`, and `busy_buffer`?) in the plan output, and gate must verify it's specified.

---

## Rollout strategy — shadow mode then cutover

This is a production control loop with money burn and destructive actions. Cutover happens behind a feature flag, gated on observed divergence behavior.

### Phase A — Shadow deployment (cycle ordering matters)

- Reconciler ships with `shadow_mode=True` (env var: `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=shadow|authoritative`).
- **Cycle ordering — explicit, not implicit:** at the start of each orchestrator cycle, after lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers` etc.) but **before** the legacy `_handle_early_termination` and `_calculate_scaling_decision`, capture an immutable `Observed` snapshot. Pass that frozen snapshot to the shadow reconciler. Let the legacy controller proceed with its normal logic on its own snapshot. Compare outcomes afterward.

  This ordering is non-negotiable: if the shadow runs after legacy mutations, divergence data is contaminated by what legacy already did, and the audit becomes meaningless.

- Shadow does `OBSERVE → PLAN → ADMIT → RECORD`. **It does NOT call `act()` in shadow mode.** Intent rows are written with `shadow=true`.
- Existing two-controller path continues to be authoritative. Nothing user-facing changes.
- A divergence audit query becomes the validation surface:
  ```sql
  -- Shadow's intended actions per cycle vs. legacy's actual outcomes per cycle, last 24h
  SELECT
    sl.created_at::date,
    sl.metadata->>'pool' AS pool,
    sl.metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' AS shadow_action,
    -- join against actual worker-row mutations from legacy controller
    ...
  FROM system_logs sl
  WHERE sl.source_type = 'orchestrator_gpu'
    AND sl.metadata ? 'shadow_intended_action'
    AND sl.created_at > now() - interval '24 hours';
  ```
  (Planner should write the full join against the `workers` table mutations or against a parallel `legacy_actions` log if one exists.)

### Phase B — Cutover decision criteria

Cutover from shadow to authoritative requires ALL of:

1. **≥24 hours of shadow mode in production traffic AND ≥N meaningful capacity events.** "Meaningful event" = a cycle where shadow_desired ≠ legacy_action OR shadow proposed `CancelPendingSpawn` OR backoff suppressed a spawn. N = at least 50; if 24h of normal traffic does not produce 50 such events, extend shadow window until they accumulate.
2. **Replay validation against captured production state.** Before cutover, the planner must produce a replay test that loads a captured snapshot of `workers` + `tasks` from the actual 2026-05-13 incident window (or a similar pathological window) and runs the reconciler against it. Assert: at the moment `queued` momentarily drops to 0 with active work in flight, the reconciler does NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn` for the spawning worker. This is the **non-negotiable proof of correctness**.
3. **No divergence in scenarios where the legacy controller's behavior is correct.** If shadow says spawn while legacy correctly says terminate-idle on a genuinely-idle worker, that's a reconciler bug, not a legacy bug — fix and re-shadow.

### Phase C — Cutover

- Flip `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=authoritative`. Reconciler becomes authoritative; legacy controller becomes a no-op stub kept for one more day.
- **At the moment of flag flip**, the reconciler's first OBSERVE will discover spawning workers with no prior intent row (they were spawned by legacy). Apply rule 9 above (synthetic adoption intent).
- Watch `scaling-audit --hours 6` output for 4 hours post-cutover. Effective utilisation should rise from baseline.
- 24h later: delete legacy stub, delete shadow-mode logging, delete the duplicate mixin code path.

---

## Deliverables (acceptance criteria)

The plan must produce, and execute must ship:

1. **Migration:** `worker_capacity_intents` table + indexes in `sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`, registered with the orchestrator's existing migration system.
2. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`** + supporting modules (`capacity_plan.py` for pure PLAN computation, types/dataclasses for `CapacityPlan` / `Action` / `Observed`).
3. **`_handle_early_termination` deleted** from `control_loop.py`. Verified by grep returning zero matches.
4. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree deleted** (and any tests that exercised it removed or rewritten against the new reconciler).
5. **Single-writer lease/lock mechanism** for authoritative mode (advisory lock or `orchestrator_leases` table; planner picks, defends choice in plan output).
6. **Cycle-trace simulation tests** under `tests/control/` covering:
   - **Test A — Queued snapshot gate:** Queued drops to zero mid-render while 1 active worker has 5 in-progress segments and a spawning replacement is in flight. Reconciler must NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn`. Spawning worker survives.
   - **Test B — Spawn backoff with fake clock:** Use a fake/frozen clock. RunPod returns `spawn_failed` 4 times in succession. Assert exact `next_spawn_allowed_at` after each failure (30s, 60s, 120s, 240s). After the clock advances past `next_spawn_allowed_at`, a fifth attempt succeeds and `consecutive_spawn_failures` resets to 0. **Required: a `Clock` injection seam** in the reconciler — no `datetime.now()` called directly inside `plan()` or `admit()`.
   - **Test C — Time-based hysteresis on cancel:** Spawning worker has been spawning for 250s when `desired_capacity` drops from 2 to 1. Reconciler must NOT immediately cancel. Cancellation may only emit when desired ≤ effective has held for ≥ `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time across at least two snapshots, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Test both branches.
   - **Test D — Route-stale workers:** A worker with `is_route_stale=True` (per `worker_state.py:83` definition based on `worker_contract_version` and route contract compatibility) is present in the worker list. Reconciler must NOT count it toward `effective_capacity` when satisfying a fresh-route `desired`.
   - **Test E — Cutover adoption:** Pre-existing spawning worker rows with no intent-table predecessor are correctly adopted on first authoritative `observe()`. They are not cancelled. A synthetic adoption intent row is written.
   - **Test F — Replay against captured incident state:** Load fixture state representing the 2026-05-13 14:11-14:14 window (or equivalent synthetic pathological state). Run reconciler. Assert no oscillation, no premature cancel.
7. **Shadow-mode infrastructure:** `shadow_mode` flag, immutable `Observed` snapshot captured at the correct point in the cycle (before legacy capacity actions), shadow logging via `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` + metadata tag, divergence audit SQL committed to `scripts/` or `sql/`.
8. **`scaling-audit` debug command extended** in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` to read `worker_capacity_intents` and report on shadow vs. authoritative cycles, divergence counts, and per-pool intent stability. Cross-repo touch; small and well-scoped.
9. **Feature-flag cutover plumbing:** `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE` env var controls shadow vs. authoritative. No-op stub of legacy controller for 24h post-cutover.

## Out of scope (do not touch in this sprint)

- **Cross-pool intent reasoning.** Each pool reconciled independently. Cross-pool rebalancing is a separate brief.
- **Simplifying `min_active_gpus` / `machines_to_keep_idle` / `buffer_based`.** Stays as input to `plan()`. The plan output must *specify* the composition (precedence order), but not change the input semantics.
- **UI / dashboard for the intent table.** `SELECT` queries are the interface.
- **Worker-side code (`reigh-worker/`).** Untouched.
- **App-side code (`reigh-app/`).** Only the cross-repo touch is extending `scaling-audit` debug command, which is small and well-scoped.
- **Replacing the existing config/feature-flag system.** Use whatever's already there.
- **Flipping production to the `control/phases/` mixin path.** Out of scope. The mixins are being deleted, not promoted.

## Watch items (gate signals)

The gate phase MUST verify each of these before allowing execute to proceed:

- **W1: Concurrency on `worker_capacity_intents` writes.** Multiple orchestrator replicas could exist (Railway autoscaling). Planner must specify uniqueness strategy: `UNIQUE (pool, cycle_id)` constraint, advisory lock around the write, or accept duplicates and let queries dedupe. Gate verifies the choice is defensible.
- **W2: Cutover sequencing.** What happens to in-flight spawning workers at the moment of `shadow_mode=False` flip? Rule 9 specifies adoption; gate verifies the implementation actually does it.
- **W3: Duplicate code path deletion completeness.** Verify by grep that `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` is removed AND no production code path imports from it AND any test rewrites are passing.
- **W4: Idempotency under cycle restart.** If `act()` partially completes (spawned one of two pods, then process crashes), the next cycle must not double-spawn. Rule 7 addresses this; gate verifies the implementation.
- **W5: Test fidelity.** Test F's captured-state fixture must be realistic. Planner should examine actual `workers` + `tasks` snapshots from the incident window if available, or synthetic state that reproduces the failure mode.
- **W6: Single-writer semantics.** Verify the advisory lock or lease implementation prevents two Railway replicas from acting on the same pool's intent simultaneously. Test by spawning two reconciler instances in the test harness against the same DB.
- **W7: Migration registration.** Verify the new migration in `sql/` is wired into whatever runner the orchestrator uses (likely a script invoked at Railway deploy time or referenced from `railway.json`). Migration must be idempotent.
- **W8: Cutover adoption test passes.** Test E above must pass before any cutover plan is signed off. Pre-existing spawning workers must not be orphaned or cancelled.

---

## Risk: what would falsify the architectural premise

If during prep or plan the planner discovers that the "two formulas computing different `desired`" diagnosis is wrong — e.g., the early-term and scale-up phases actually coordinate correctly through some mechanism this brief overlooked — **stop and report**. Do not proceed with deleting `_handle_early_termination` based on a faulty premise.

Less drastically: if the planner finds that the `CapacityPlan` abstraction can't cleanly express some real-world scenario in the existing `worker_state.py` decision logic (around lines 588-780), surface that during critique. The right answer might be a different decomposition than OBSERVE/PLAN/ADMIT/ACT/RECORD.

The whole point of running this at `high` author depth is so the planner can push back on the brief's design if it's wrong.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Unify Capacity Control via Durable-Intent Reconciler (v2)

## Overview

The orchestrator currently runs two parallel capacity controllers per cycle: `_handle_early_termination` (`control_loop.py:343–427`) and `_calculate_scaling_decision` → `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` (`worker_state.py:556–676`). They emit opposing actions against the same phase-1 snapshot, producing the 4 %‑utilisation churn observed on 2026‑05‑13.

The brief's "one durable-intent reconciler" architecture remains the right answer; the v1 plan had five concrete defects that prevent the design from actually working as written:

1. **Postgres advisory locks are useless across PostgREST.** Each `supabase.rpc()` / `.execute()` call is its own HTTP request and its own transaction, so `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` releases immediately. Single-writer must move inside an **RPC that owns the whole admit+record transaction**.
2. **`UNIQUE(pool, cycle_id)` is fragile.** `OrchestratorControlLoop.cycle_count` resets to 0 on process restart (`control_loop.py:52`), so two replicas / two deploys produce colliding rows. And v1's "INSERT for backoff update" violates the constraint outright.
3. **Backoff trigger surface is too narrow.** `termination_reason='spawn_failed'` is set only inside `_spawn_worker` (`control_loop.py:1395, 1428`); lifecycle-failure reasons (`gpu_not_detected`, `ready_not_claiming_timeout`, `startup_timeout`) come from `_handle_spawning_workers` and never flow into `consecutive_spawn_failures` if the reconciler only watches synchronous spawn return values.
4. **Spawn surface naming mismatch.** v1 named `self.runpod.create_pod(...)`; production spawn is `WorkerSpawnerAdapter.spawn_worker` (`worker_spawner.py:53`), and the DB-row + spawn_failed bookkeeping lives in `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` (`control_loop.py:1371`). The reconciler must reuse that primitive, not invent a parallel one.
5. **Residual legacy `desired` in `_execute_scaling`.** v1 kept the idle-timeout scale-down path consuming `decision.workers_to_terminate` from the legacy `calculate_scaling_decision_pure`. That keeps one of the two conflicting formulas alive post-cutover.

v2 replaces v1's single-writer mechanism with a leases table + reconciler RPC, splits mutable backoff state out of the append-only audit table, broadens the backoff trigger to all spawning-phase failure reasons, points the reconciler at the existing `_spawn_worker` primitive, and feeds the reconciler's `desired` into `_execute_scaling` so the legacy formula stops being authoritative anywhere post-cutover.

Repo shape that matters:
- Production runtime: `gpu_orchestrator/main.py` → `OrchestratorControlLoop.run_single_cycle()` (`control_loop.py:89`).
- DB integration boundary: `gpu_orchestrator/database.py:138` (`DatabaseClient`, Supabase service-role over PostgREST).
- Spawn primitive: `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` (`control_loop.py:1371`); RunPod adapter at `gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py:53`.
- Pure scaling math (to be retired with the legacy controllers): `worker_state.py:556`.
- Logger handler: `gpu_orchestrator/database_log_handler.py:144–182` — caller extras land at `metadata.extra.*`, NOT at the top level of `metadata`. Direct-INSERT is required for top-level metadata keys.
- Migration runners: `scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` (hardcoded list, lines 39–44), `scripts/setup_database.py`, `scripts/show_migrations.py` — all three may enumerate migrations and must be checked.
- Mixin tree confirmed test-only: only `tests/` imports `control.phases`.

---

## Phase 1: Foundation — Schema, RPC, Config, Types

### Step 1: Migration — split audit and mutable state (`sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`, `sql/20260514000001_create_orchestrator_leases.sql`, `sql/20260514000002_create_worker_capacity_state.sql`, `sql/20260514000003_func_reconcile_capacity.sql`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **`worker_capacity_intents`** — append-only audit, no uniqueness constraint on `(pool, cycle_id)`. Replace `cycle_id bigint` with `cycle_uuid uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()` plus `orchestrator_instance_id text NOT NULL` and `cycle_seq bigint NOT NULL` (the local `cycle_count`, useful for log correlation but not unique across processes). Indexes: `(pool, route_key, created_at DESC)` and `(orchestrator_instance_id, cycle_seq)`.
2. **`worker_capacity_state`** — mutable per-`(pool, route_key)` row holding backoff:
   ```sql
   CREATE TABLE public.worker_capacity_state (
     pool                        text NOT NULL,
     route_key                   text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
     consecutive_spawn_failures  int  NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
     last_spawn_failure_reason   text,
     last_spawn_failure_at       timestamptz,
     next_spawn_allowed_at       timestamptz,
     last_successful_spawn_at    timestamptz,
     stable_since                timestamptz,
     last_desired                int,
     updated_at                  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
     PRIMARY KEY (pool, route_key)
   );
   ```
   `UPSERT` target; mutated transactionally inside the reconciler RPC.
3. **`orchestrator_leases`** — single-writer lease per `(scope_kind, scope_key)`:
   ```sql
   CREATE TABLE public.orchestrator_leases (
     scope_kind   text NOT NULL,            -- 'capacity_reconciler'
     scope_key    text NOT NULL,            -- pool name
     holder_id    text NOT NULL,            -- orchestrator_instance_id
     acquired_at  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
     expires_at   timestamptz NOT NULL,
     PRIMARY KEY (scope_kind, scope_key)
   );
   ```
4. **`func_reconcile_capacity(payload jsonb) RETURNS jsonb`** — the single-writer transaction. Acquires/refreshes the lease (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (scope_kind, scope_key) DO UPDATE SET holder_id=..., expires_at=... WHERE orchestrator_leases.expires_at < now() OR orchestrator_leases.holder_id = EXCLUDED.holder_id RETURNING holder_id = $caller`), upserts `worker_capacity_state`, inserts a `worker_capacity_intents` row, and returns a JSON object: `{lease_acquired: bool, admitted_actions: [...], state: {...}}`. **All in one transaction.** This is the only construct that gives PostgREST-correct single-writer semantics. (Resolves FLAG-001, correctness-1, all_locations-2.)
5. **All four files** use `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` / `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` for idempotency. **Register each** in `scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` (lines 39–44), `scripts/setup_database.py`, and `scripts/show_migrations.py` (audit those latter two for separate migration lists — if they enumerate independently, append the new files; if they share state with `apply_sql_migrations.py`, one edit suffices). (Resolves all_locations-1.)
6. **Deploy-time hook.** Add `gpu_orchestrator/railway.json` `deploy.releaseCommand: "python -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations"` (or, if Railway's release-command primitive isn't available on this plan, wrap the Dockerfile `CMD` with `python -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations && python -m gpu_orchestrator.main continuous`). Verify against the existing `gpu_orchestrator/railway.json` (separate from the repo-root `railway.json` which builds `api_orchestrator/Dockerfile`). (Resolves issue_hints-1, W7.)

### Step 2: Extend `OrchestratorConfig` (`gpu_orchestrator/config.py:43`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** to `OrchestratorConfig` and `from_env()` (line 114):
   - `capacity_reconciler_mode: str` — `"off" | "shadow" | "authoritative"` (env `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE`, default `"off"`).
   - `spawn_failure_backoff_base_sec: int` (default 30), `spawn_failure_backoff_max_sec: int` (default 600).
   - `spawn_failure_lookback_minutes: int` (default 15) — window for deriving `consecutive_spawn_failures` from worker history.
   - `cancel_hysteresis_min_seconds: int` — default `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec`.
   - `orchestrator_instance_id: str` — `os.getenv("ORCHESTRATOR_INSTANCE_ID")` or `socket.gethostname() + "/" + uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]` at startup. Stable for the process lifetime.
2. **Log** the new fields in `log_config()` (line 214).

### Step 3: Reconciler types (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_types.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Frozen dataclasses**:
   - `Observed(cycle_uuid, cycle_seq, taken_at, pool, route_key, active_fresh, active_stale, spawning_fresh, spawning_stale, idle, busy, queued, in_progress, workers_by_id)`.
   - `Action`: `Spawn(pool, route_key, count, reason)`, `CancelPendingSpawn(worker_id, reason)`, `Noop(reason)`. (Active-worker termination stays in `_execute_scaling` — see Step 8.)
   - `BackoffState(consecutive_spawn_failures, next_spawn_allowed_at, last_spawn_failure_reason)`.
   - `CapacityPlan(desired, effective_capacity, actions, reason, valid_until, stable_since)`.
   - `ReconciliationResult(plan, admitted_actions, suppressed, intent_row_id, mode, lease_acquired)`.
2. **`Clock` protocol** (`now() -> datetime`) — `plan()` and `admit()` MUST NOT call `datetime.now()` directly. (Test B fixture seam.)
3. **`route_key`** derivation: `f"{worker_backend}:{worker_profile}:{selector_namespace}:{selector_version or 'default'}:v{worker_contract_version}"` — from `OrchestratorConfig` for the current route, from `DerivedWorkerState` (fields at `worker_state.py:71–77`) for individual workers.

---

## Phase 2: Reconciler Implementation

### Step 4: Pure PLAN computation (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_plan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **`compute_plan(observed, prior_intent, backoff, config, clock) -> CapacityPlan`** — no I/O, no `datetime.now()`.
2. **Single `desired` formula — precedence written down at the top of the file** (Resolves brief §"Out of scope" precedence requirement):
   ```
   current_capacity   = active_fresh + spawning_fresh             # stale-route workers do NOT count toward fresh desired
   workers_covering   = idle_fresh   + spawning_fresh             # capable of taking a queued task
   uncovered_tasks    = max(0, queued - workers_covering)
   spawn_for_tasks    = uncovered_tasks
   floor              = max(min_active_gpus, busy_fresh + max(machines_to_keep_idle, 1))
   capacity_after_t   = current_capacity + spawn_for_tasks
   spawn_for_floor    = max(0, floor - capacity_after_t)
   min_gap            = max(0, min_active_gpus - capacity_after_t)
   spawn_for_floor    = max(spawn_for_floor, min_gap)
   desired            = min(current_capacity + spawn_for_tasks + spawn_for_floor, max_active_gpus)
   effective_capacity = active_fresh + spawning_fresh - cancels_in_admit
   ```
3. **Cancel semantics (invariants 2, 3):**
   - Never emit `CancelPendingSpawn` when `observed.queued > 0` (invariant 2).
   - Emit `CancelPendingSpawn` only when ALL: (a) `desired <= effective_capacity - 1`, (b) `prior_intent and clock.now() - prior_intent.stable_since >= config.cancel_hysteresis_min_seconds`, (c) `prior_intent.desired_capacity <= prior_intent.observed_active + prior_intent.observed_spawning` (two-snapshot proof) OR `clock.now() >= prior_intent.valid_until`. Wall-clock, not cycle-count.
4. **`valid_until = clock.now() + 2 * config.orchestrator_poll_sec`.**
5. **No active-worker termination action** is emitted by `plan()`. Active scale-down stays under `_execute_scaling` (invariant 4) but its `desired` input changes in Step 8.

### Step 5: Reconciler service (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **`observe()`** — async. Reads `workers` + task counts (reusing `_fetch_current_state` shape from `control_loop.py:170`). Frozen `Observed`. **Also derives `BackoffState` from observed worker history**, not from synchronous spawn returns:
   - `recent_failures = db.get_recent_spawning_failures(pool, route_key, since=now - spawn_failure_lookback_minutes)`. Counts workers whose final `termination_reason ∈ {'spawn_failed', 'gpu_not_detected', 'ready_not_claiming_timeout', 'startup_timeout', 'spawning_timeout', 'No Runpod ID'}` (sourced from grep of `_handle_spawning_workers` / `_mark_worker_error` reasons in `control_loop.py`). The reason set is config-driven; add new reasons by editing one constant in `capacity_reconciler.py`.
   - `consecutive_spawn_failures` is the count of consecutive failures since the most recent successful promotion-to-active for `(pool, route_key)`. **This is the same derivation in shadow and authoritative mode** — resolves correctness-6 (shadow now observes suppression events realistically). (Resolves FLAG-003, correctness-4, correctness-6.)
2. **`plan(observed, backoff)`** — calls `compute_plan` from Step 4. Loads `prior_intent` for the pool via `db.get_latest_intent(pool, route_key)`.
3. **`admit(plan, backoff)`** — drops `Spawn` actions when `clock.now() < backoff.next_spawn_allowed_at`; produces `suppressed=[(action, reason, next_allowed_at), ...]`.
4. **`act(admitted, observed, shadow)`** — two modes:
   - **Shadow:** for each admitted/suppressed action, **direct-INSERT** a `system_logs` row via `db.insert_log({'source_type': 'orchestrator_gpu', 'log_level': 'INFO', 'message': ..., 'metadata': {'shadow_intended_action': True, 'action_type': ..., 'pool': ..., 'route_key': ..., 'desired': ..., 'effective': ..., ...}})`. **Bypasses the Python logger handler** so the metadata key lives at the top level (audit-SQL friendly). The logger path stuffs caller extras under `metadata.extra` (`database_log_handler.py:182`) and would break the audit query if used. (Resolves issue_hints-2.) No RunPod calls, no worker-row mutations.
   - **Authoritative:** call the **reconciler RPC `func_reconcile_capacity`** with the planned action set in one transaction (lease+state+intent). If the RPC returns `lease_acquired=False`, log `single_writer_skipped` and return — the other replica owns this cycle. If `lease_acquired=True`, RPC has already written the intent row; only THEN dispatch per-action side effects:
     - `Spawn(count=N)`: call `self._spawn_one_worker(...)` N times. The implementation **delegates to the existing spawn primitive**, refactored out of `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` (`control_loop.py:1371`) into a free function `gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py::spawn_one_worker(db, runpod_adapter, config, intent_row_id)`. Both the reconciler and the legacy `_execute_scaling` call site (currently `control_loop.py:1037`) route through this same function so spawn_failed bookkeeping and DB row creation are not duplicated. (Resolves correctness-3, callers-2.)
     - `CancelPendingSpawn(worker_id)`: call `await self.runpod.terminate_worker(runpod_id)` + `db.update_worker_status(..., 'terminated', metadata_update)`. Same primitive currently used at `control_loop.py:410`.
5. **`record(...)`** — purely a no-op in v2: the RPC has already written the intent row in authoritative mode. In **shadow mode**, the reconciler **does its own INSERT** into `worker_capacity_intents` with `shadow=true` (no lease, no state mutation), satisfying invariant 6 (one row per pool per cycle, both modes).
6. **Invariant 7 idempotency:** because the RPC writes the intent row inside the same transaction as the lease+state mutation, a crash between the RPC and the side-effect dispatch leaves the intent row recorded. The next `observe()` finds the spawning workers (still being created may be incomplete; OK — they will appear or not, and the next cycle's plan adjusts). Spawn DB rows are created inside `spawn_one_worker` BEFORE the RunPod call (matches existing `_spawn_worker` ordering at lines 1380–1395), so a crash between DB-row insert and RunPod call leaves an orphan `worker` row that the existing `_handle_spawning_workers` (`control_loop.py:433`) marks `spawn_failed` on next cycle. No double-spawn. (Resolves invariant 7 / W4.)

### Step 6: Database access (`gpu_orchestrator/database.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** thin methods on `DatabaseClient`:
   - `get_latest_intent(pool: str, route_key: str | None) -> dict | None` — direct table read, ORDER BY `created_at DESC` LIMIT 1.
   - `get_recent_spawning_failures(pool, route_key, since) -> list[dict]` — direct table read against `workers` filtering on `termination_reason` set.
   - `reconcile_capacity_rpc(payload: dict) -> dict` — calls `func_reconcile_capacity`. **This is the single-writer entry point.**
   - `insert_log(entry: dict) -> None` — direct INSERT into `system_logs`, bypassing the Python logger (for shadow intended-action rows). Validates `source_type ∈ {'orchestrator_gpu','orchestrator_api','worker'}` client-side so a typo can't 500 the cycle.
   - `insert_shadow_intent(row: dict) -> uuid` — shadow-mode direct INSERT into `worker_capacity_intents`.
2. **RLS:** service-role key bypasses RLS, but new tables should still have `ALTER TABLE ... ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;` + a service-role policy for hygiene. Add to migration (Step 1). (Resolves all_locations-2.)

---

## Phase 3: Wire Into the Control Loop

### Step 7: Capture snapshot and run reconciler (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **`__init__`** (line 44): when `config.capacity_reconciler_mode != "off"`, instantiate `self.reconciler = CapacityReconciler(self.db, self.config, self.runpod, clock=SystemClock(), spawn_one_worker=spawn_one_worker)`.
2. **`run_single_cycle`** (line 89): after Phase 2 (`_derive_all_worker_states`, line 113) and BEFORE Phase 3 `_handle_early_termination` (line 116) and Phase 8 `_calculate_scaling_decision` (line 131), build the frozen `Observed` snapshot from `worker_states + task_counts`. Pass to `await self.reconciler.reconcile(cycle_seq=self.cycle_count, observed=...)`. Stash the result on `self._last_reconciliation` for Step 8.
3. **Shadow mode:** legacy phases 3 and 8 continue to run authoritatively. Reconciler logs + records.
4. **Authoritative mode:** skip the call to `_handle_early_termination` (line 116) and skip the spawn path inside `_execute_scaling` (line 994 onward — `if decision.should_scale_up: ...`). Idle-timeout scale-down for active workers stays (invariant 4), with the modification in Step 8.

### Step 8: Feed reconciler `desired` into idle-timeout path (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py:943` `_execute_scaling`)
**Scope:** Small

1. In authoritative mode, **replace** the inputs to the idle-timeout sizing block at `control_loop.py:978–983`:
   ```python
   # authoritative mode (reconciler is source of truth for desired)
   reconciled = self._last_reconciliation  # ReconciliationResult or None
   if reconciled is not None:
       desired = reconciled.plan.desired
       current_cap = reconciled.plan.effective_capacity
   else:
       desired, current_cap = decision.desired_workers, decision.current_capacity  # fallback for "off" mode
   max_by_capacity = max(0, current_cap - desired)
   ```
   `max_by_buffer` and `max_by_min_active` stay the same (they don't depend on the legacy `desired`). This makes the reconciler's `desired` the sole authority post-cutover. (Resolves FLAG-006, correctness-5, scope.)
2. Note in a comment that `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` is still computed in shadow mode for parity comparison but its `desired_workers` field is unused in authoritative mode and the function is slated for deletion in Phase D below. (Resolves callers-1 dead-state note.)

### Step 9: Refactor spawn primitive (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py:1371`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extract** the body of `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` (`control_loop.py:1371–~1440`) into a free async function `spawn_one_worker(db, runpod_adapter, config, *, intent_row_id: uuid | None = None, queued_count: int = 0) -> bool` in `gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`.
2. **`_spawn_worker`** becomes a thin wrapper that calls the free function with `self.db`, `self.runpod`, `self.config`. The reconciler calls the free function directly. spawn_failed bookkeeping lives in one place.
3. Add an optional `intent_row_id` linkage column to the `workers.metadata` JSON so audit queries can join workers back to their authorising intent.

### Step 10: Delete legacy capacity logic (Phase D, separate commit in the same PR)
**Scope:** Small

1. After 24h of authoritative running, in a **separate revertible commit**: delete `_handle_early_termination` (`control_loop.py:343–427`), delete `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` and its callers, delete the spawn paths in `_execute_scaling`. Verify `grep -n '_handle_early_termination' gpu_orchestrator/` returns zero.
2. Delete `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` and the four mixin-facing tests:
   - `tests/test_direct_control_health.py`
   - `tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_sprint7_staged_rollback_fixture.py`
   - `tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_sprint10_canary_rollback.py`
   - `tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_sprint12_staging_flip.py`
   plus `tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` in its entirety. Confirm `grep -rn 'control\.phases' gpu_orchestrator/ tests/` returns zero. (Resolves W3.)

---

## Phase 4: Tests, Fixture Capture, and Tooling

### Step 11: Capture real incident state for Test F (`tests/control/fixtures/incident_20260513_1411.json`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run a one-shot script** (`scripts/dump_incident_fixture.py`, new) against the production Supabase using the service-role key to extract `SELECT * FROM workers WHERE updated_at BETWEEN '2026-05-13 14:11:00Z' AND '2026-05-13 14:14:00Z'` plus the contemporaneous `tasks` snapshot. Save as JSON.
2. If the production DB no longer holds rows from that window (Supabase retention varies), fall back to the next-closest pathological window in `system_logs` (filter on `metadata->>'termination_reason' = 'early_termination_over_capacity'` densest 3-minute bucket). Document which window was captured.
3. **Only if both windows are unavailable**, fall back to a synthetic fixture matching the postmortem shape (`queued=0, in_progress=6, active=1 busy, spawning=1`). This is now a documented fallback, not the default. (Resolves issue_hints-3, W5.)

### Step 12: Cycle-trace simulation tests (`tests/control/test_capacity_reconciler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Test A — Queued snapshot gate** (`queued=0, in_progress=6, active=1 busy, spawning=1` → no `CancelPendingSpawn`).
2. **Test B — Spawn backoff with `FakeClock`** (4 derived-failure events → `next_spawn_allowed_at` deltas exactly 30s, 60s, 120s, 240s; success resets to 0). Derivation reads from a mock `workers` table the test populates — this also proves Test B's input surface includes `gpu_not_detected` and `ready_not_claiming_timeout`, not just `spawn_failed`.
3. **Test C — Time-based hysteresis** (250s spawning, `desired` drops 2→1; hysteresis still open → no cancel; advance clock past `cancel_hysteresis_min_seconds` → cancel emitted; also test `valid_until` elapsed branch).
4. **Test D — Route-stale workers** (`is_route_stale=True` worker does not count toward `effective_capacity` for fresh route).
5. **Test E — Cutover adoption** (pre-existing spawning workers with no intent row are not cancelled on first authoritative cycle; synthetic adoption intent recorded).
6. **Test F — Replay against captured incident state** (load fixture from Step 11; assert no oscillation, no premature cancel across 5 simulated cycles).
7. **Test G — Single-writer via RPC** (spin up two `CapacityReconciler` instances against a Postgres test container, both call `reconcile_capacity_rpc` for the same pool in the same cycle; assert exactly one `lease_acquired=True`, the other returns `False` and emits zero side effects). The lease + intent INSERT must be in one transaction — verifiable by killing the connection mid-RPC and confirming neither lease nor intent row persists.
8. **Test H — Idempotency under crash** (kill the reconciler process between RPC return and `spawn_one_worker` call; next cycle's `observe()` notes spawning workers in progress and does NOT exceed `desired`). Achievable by injecting a raise after the RPC returns and re-running.
9. **Test I — Shadow audit-key visibility** (insert a shadow log via `db.insert_log`; assert `SELECT metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' FROM system_logs WHERE ...` returns `'true'`, NOT NULL — proves the direct-INSERT path keeps the key top-level). (Resolves issue_hints-2.)
10. **Test J — Backoff observes lifecycle failures** (populate `workers` table with three rows whose `termination_reason ∈ {'gpu_not_detected', 'ready_not_claiming_timeout', 'spawn_failed'}` in lookback window; assert `consecutive_spawn_failures == 3`). (Resolves FLAG-003 evidence.)

### Step 13: Validation
**Scope:** Small

1. **Unit:** `pytest tests/control/test_capacity_plan.py -v` — pure PLAN tests, no DB, no clock.
2. **Integration:** `pytest tests/control/test_capacity_reconciler.py -v` — Tests A–J.
3. **Full suite:** `pytest tests/ -v` after mixin deletion (Phase D commit).
4. **Migration smoke:** `python scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` twice against a throwaway schema; assert idempotency on all four new files.
5. **Two-replica RPC test:** stand up a local Postgres (Docker), apply migrations, run Test G's two-reconciler scenario against it.
6. **Shadow-mode local boot:** orchestrator with `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=shadow` against staging DB for ~10 cycles. Verify (a) shadow intent rows with `shadow=true`, (b) zero RunPod calls from the reconciler, (c) audit SQL `SELECT metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' FROM system_logs WHERE ... ` returns non-NULL rows.
7. **Authoritative staging cutover:** flip flag, verify synthetic adoption for pre-existing spawning workers (Test E in vivo).
8. **Production cutover gates** (per brief Phase B): ≥24h shadow, ≥50 meaningful divergence events, replay validation against the Step-11 captured incident state.

### Step 14: Cross-repo `scaling-audit` is out of scope for THIS PR
**Scope:** None (deliberate)

1. The brief calls for extending `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py:scaling-audit` to read `worker_capacity_intents`. That file is in a different repo and outside this PR's mechanical reach.
2. **In this PR:** commit the divergence audit SQL to `sql/audit_capacity_intent_divergence.sql` and a stand-alone read-only helper `scripts/scaling_audit_cli.py` in THIS repo that the operator can run locally against staging/prod. This is enough to validate Phase B cutover gates from this repo alone.
3. **Open a separate ticket** for `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` extension; reference the SQL committed here. Acceptance criterion for the `reigh-app` touch is demoted to `info` because the executor here cannot run it. (Resolves all_locations-3.)

---

## Execution Order

1. **Schema + RPC + types** (Steps 1–3) — RPC is the single-writer foundation; everything else depends on it.
2. **Pure PLAN + reconciler service** (Steps 4–6) — Step 4 is fully testable without DB.
3. **Spawn primitive refactor** (Step 9) — must land before reconciler `act()` can call it.
4. **Control-loop wiring** (Steps 7–8) — shadow mode is observable in prod with zero behavioural risk; Step 8 only activates when authoritative.
5. **Fixture capture** (Step 11) — needs production DB access; do early so Test F can land green.
6. **Tests** (Step 12) — all of A–J must pass before authoritative cutover.
7. **Authoritative cutover** — flag flip; legacy stays as a no-op for 24h.
8. **Legacy deletion** (Step 10) — separate commit, after 24h authoritative bake.

## Validation Order

1. `pytest` on `tests/control/test_capacity_plan.py` (pure, fastest signal).
2. `pytest` on `tests/control/test_capacity_reconciler.py` Tests A–F + I–J (mock DB / Postgres test container).
3. Test G + H (two-replica + idempotency) against local Postgres.
4. Migration idempotency (`scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` × 2).
5. Full `pytest tests/` after Phase-D mixin deletion.
6. Shadow-mode staging dry run, ≥24h, ≥50 meaningful events.
7. Replay validation against captured incident fixture (Step 11).
8. Authoritative staging cutover; in-vivo adoption verification.
9. Production cutover; effective-utilisation ≥70% over 24h.

        Gate signals:
        {
  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 2,
    "idea": "# brief-unify-capacity-control-20260513\n\n**Profile:** `all-codex/standard/high`\n**Repo:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator` (Python, Railway-deployed) + small cross-repo touch in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py`\n**Branch base:** `origin/main` at `fcec14f`\n**Rollout:** shadow-mode \u2192 divergence audit \u2192 flag-flip cutover\n\n> **Brief revision history:** drafted 2026-05-13. Sense-checked by Codex GPT-5.5 `high` (read-only, repo-aware). Corrections from that review folded in below \u2014 most importantly: code-level claims about `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` and the cycle phase order have been corrected against ground truth.\n\n---\n\n## Goal\n\nReplace the orchestrator's two-controller scaling loop with one durable-intent reconciler. Specifically:\n\n1. Introduce `worker_capacity_intents` (Postgres) as the durable source of truth for \"what capacity should exist right now, for what reason, with what cooldowns.\"\n2. Build `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py` with an explicit five-phase state machine: **OBSERVE \u2192 PLAN \u2192 ADMIT \u2192 ACT \u2192 RECORD**.\n3. Delete `_handle_early_termination` entirely. Its job becomes a `cancel_pending_spawn` action emitted by the reconciler under strictly-defined conditions.\n4. Fold RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff into the same reconciler as admission control (no separate guardrail path).\n5. Delete the test-only duplicate code path under `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` (which is *not* the production runtime path \u2014 see \"Context\" below).\n6. Roll out via shadow mode: new reconciler runs alongside the existing controller, logs intended actions, doesn't take effect until a feature flag flips.\n\nSuccess is measured by **production effective-utilisation rising from ~4% to \u226570%** sustained over 24h post-cutover, with no user-task failures attributable to the new controller.\n\n---\n\n## Context \u2014 what's broken\n\nA 2-hour rollout window on 2026-05-13 observed **73 GPU workers spawned, 70 killed before becoming productive, 1 worker did all the actual work** (4% effective utilisation). The same pattern is present in git history going back at least 6 weeks.\n\nTwo parallel Codex GPT-5.5 sense-checks (independent runs, no cross-talk) reading `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` converged on a single architectural diagnosis, **subsequently verified by a third Codex `high` review** against the actual code:\n\n> The orchestrator runs **two independent capacity controllers** in one cycle. `_handle_early_termination` (around `control_loop.py:340`, `current_capacity` derived at `:362`, gate check at `:377`) computes one `desired` figure from `task_counts.total * scale_down_multiplier` + min GPUs + idle buffer. Later in the same cycle, `_calculate_scaling_decision` calls `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` at `control_loop.py:802` (when `use_new_scaling_logic` is enabled), which emits spawn action math from queued coverage (`workers_covering_tasks = idle + spawning`, `uncovered_tasks = queued - workers_covering_tasks`, `spawn_for_tasks = uncovered_tasks`, see `worker_state.py:588`) plus a busy-worker buffer floor (`worker_state.py:594`). **However**, `desired_workers` itself is still populated from the legacy `total_workload * scale_up_multiplier`, `busy + idle buffer`, and `min_active_gpus` block at `worker_state.py:616`.\n>\n> The bug is **not** \"stale snapshot between phases\" \u2014 the scaling decision is computed *after* early-term, not before. The bug is that early-term **mutates the worker list** (terminating spawning workers) before a second, separate scaling formula runs against the same phase-1 task snapshot and the now-changed worker list. Two formulas, two notions of `desired`, one cycle, opposing actions.\n\nThree named failure modes ride on this defect:\n\n- **Spawn churn from snapshot gates.** `_handle_early_termination` is guarded by `if task_counts.queued > 0` (at `control_loop.py:377`). `task_counts.queued` is an instantaneous snapshot taken once at the top of each cycle. When the single productive worker had just claimed a segment (queue momentarily empty, six in-progress segments active), early-term fired and killed the spawning replacement. Next cycle re-spawned. Loop.\n- **No backoff for RunPod `spawn_failed`.** 55 of the 73 deaths were RunPod refusing pod allocation. The orchestrator retried at 30-second cycle cadence indefinitely.\n- **Mutating actions sequenced before mismatched re-derivation.** Early-term terminates spawning workers from the worker list, then `_calculate_scaling_decision` re-derives `desired` against the same phase-1 task snapshot but a now-shorter worker list, and decides to re-spawn what early-term just killed.\n\nA team-authored postmortem (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-postmortem-20260513.md`) and approach memo (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-approach-20260513.md`) cover the symptom-level analysis. The architectural prescription below is the *deep* fix.\n\n### Duplicate code path \u2014 clarified\n\n`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` is **the** production runtime path. `main.py` imports `OrchestratorControlLoop` from it. The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree contains duplicated chunks of capacity logic (including a duplicate `_handle_early_termination`) but is **only exercised by tests, not by production**. Default deletion target for this sprint: the mixin duplication under `control/phases/`. The planner may override that choice only by first showing concretely that the mixins contain logic worth migrating into `control_loop.py` (or by proposing to flip production over to the mixin path \u2014 but that is out of scope for this sprint and would be a separate brief).\n\n---\n\n## Architecture \u2014 what we're building\n\n### New Postgres table\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE public.worker_capacity_intents (\n  id                          uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n  pool                        text NOT NULL,\n  route_key                   text,                              -- nullable for pool-wide intents\n  desired_capacity            int  NOT NULL,\n  reason                      text NOT NULL,                     -- 'queued_coverage'|'min_floor'|'cooldown'|...\n  observed_queued             int,\n  observed_active             int,\n  observed_spawning           int,\n  cycle_id                    bigint,\n  created_at                  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  valid_until                 timestamptz,                       -- intent expires; absence \u2260 kill\n  stable_since                timestamptz,                       -- when desired_capacity last changed\n  consecutive_spawn_failures  int  NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\n  next_spawn_allowed_at       timestamptz,                       -- backoff cooldown\n  shadow                      boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true      -- shadow vs authoritative mode\n);\n\nCREATE INDEX worker_capacity_intents_pool_route_created\n  ON public.worker_capacity_intents (pool, route_key, created_at DESC);\n```\n\n**One row written per cycle per pool**, regardless of whether the intent changed. This is the audit trail.\n\n**Migration location:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/` (alongside the existing `2025*_*.sql` files). Filename pattern: `20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`. The repo's existing migration registration system applies \u2014 planner should locate how it's wired (likely `scripts/` or a runner referenced from Railway config) and follow the established pattern.\n\n### Logging \u2014 `system_logs` constraint\n\nExisting `system_logs.valid_source_type` CHECK constraint permits only `orchestrator_gpu`, `orchestrator_api`, and `worker`. **Do not invent a new `source_type` value.** Use `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` with a metadata tag field (e.g., `metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' = 'true'`) to distinguish shadow log entries from authoritative ones. Saves a migration and avoids breaking other readers of `system_logs`.\n\n### New Python module \u2014 `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`\n\nOwns the full five-phase state machine. Pure-where-possible, side-effects concentrated at boundaries.\n\n```python\nclass CapacityReconciler:\n    def reconcile(self, cycle_id: int) -> ReconciliationResult:\n        observed = self.observe()                     # phase 1: read workers + tasks\n        plan = self.plan(observed)                    # phase 2: compute CapacityPlan\n        admitted = self.admit(plan)                   # phase 3: filter by backoff/cooldown\n        result = self.act(admitted, shadow=self.shadow_mode)  # phase 4: side effects (or log-only)\n        self.record(cycle_id, observed, plan, admitted, result)  # phase 5: persist intent row\n        return result\n```\n\nWhere:\n\n- **`observe()`** is the canonical read of `workers` + `tasks` state. Counts active / spawning / route-stale / idle, computes `total_tasks = queued + in_progress`. Returns a frozen `Observed` dataclass \u2014 **immutable**, taken at one moment in time, passed by value through subsequent phases.\n- **`plan(observed)`** computes ONE `desired_capacity` from ONE formula. Returns a `CapacityPlan` with `desired`, `effective_capacity = active + spawning - admitted_pending_cancels`, `actions = [Spawn | CancelPendingSpawn | TerminateIdle | Noop]`, `reason`, `valid_until`.\n- **`admit(plan)`** is admission control. Checks `next_spawn_allowed_at` per pool, drops `Spawn` actions during cooldown, logs `suppressed_by_backoff`.\n- **`act(admitted, shadow)`** executes side effects when `shadow=False`. When `shadow=True`, logs intended actions to `system_logs` (source_type=`orchestrator_gpu`, metadata tag `shadow_intended_action`). **In shadow mode, `act()` MUST NOT call RunPod, MUST NOT mutate worker rows, MUST NOT update backoff state. Shadow is read-only on the external world.**\n- **`record(...)`** writes one row to `worker_capacity_intents` regardless of mode. The `shadow` boolean column distinguishes shadow rows from authoritative rows.\n\n### Rules the reconciler MUST honor (invariants)\n\nThese are invariants, not preferences. The cycle-trace simulation tests in the deliverables section exist to enforce them.\n\n1. **Spawning workers count as capacity** until they (a) report `spawn_failed`, (b) exceed `active_initializing_max_sec`, or (c) are explicitly cancelled by a `CancelPendingSpawn` action emitted by this reconciler.\n2. **Never kill or cancel a spawning worker because `queued == 0`.** The queue snapshot does not have authority over an authorized spawn.\n3. **Cancel `Spawn` action emission** only when the desired_capacity has remained \u2264 effective for **at least two consecutive observed reconciler snapshots spanning at least `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time**, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Wall-clock based, not iteration-count based \u2014 duplicate Railway replicas or delayed cycles make raw cycle count unreliable.\n4. **Scale down active workers** only via idle timeout + min/buffer constraints. Active workers are never killed by capacity-pressure logic.\n5. **RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff is keyed by `(pool, route_key)`**, not globally. `consecutive_spawn_failures += 1` and `next_spawn_allowed_at = now() + min(2^N * 30s, 600s)`. Successful spawn for that `(pool, route_key)` resets the counter to 0.\n6. **`record()` writes exactly one row per pool per cycle**, even when nothing changed. Audit trail integrity.\n7. **No authoritative spawn action executes without a prior `RECORD` write succeeding** within the same reconciliation. This means `act(shadow=False)` calls `record()` first and only then dispatches RunPod creates / cancellations. Idempotency: if `act()` partially completes and the process crashes, the next cycle sees the recorded intent and can resume.\n8. **Single-writer per pool per cycle in authoritative mode.** Use a Postgres advisory lock keyed on `hashtext(pool)` or a lease row in a small `orchestrator_leases` table, held for the duration of `admit()` + `act()`. Without this, two Railway replicas can each spawn against the same pool's intent and double-provision. (Shadow mode does not need the lock \u2014 multiple shadows are fine since they don't mutate.)\n9. **At cutover, the reconciler must adopt pre-existing spawning workers** that have no associated intent row. These were spawned by the legacy controller before cutover. Treat them as part of effective_capacity; assign a synthetic adoption intent on first observe, do not cancel them.\n\n### Phasing \u2014 what gets deleted, what survives\n\n**Deleted:**\n- `_handle_early_termination` (the entire phase 2 block in `control_loop.py` at lines ~340-400).\n- The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree (test-only duplicate code path). Includes any tests that exercised it; replace those with tests against the new reconciler.\n\n**Survives:**\n- Worker lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers`, heartbeat checks, promote-to-active logic) \u2014 stays in `control_loop.py`.\n- RunPod pod creation primitives (`runpod_client.create_pod` and friends) \u2014 stay. `act()` calls into them.\n- `min_active_gpus`, `machines_to_keep_idle`, `buffer_based` calculations \u2014 stay as **inputs** to `plan()`. **Out of scope for this sprint** is simplifying or renaming them; `plan()` consumes them and emits one authoritative `desired` from one formula. The planner MUST write down exactly that composition (what's the precedence order between `min_active_gpus` floor, `queued_coverage`, and `busy_buffer`?) in the plan output, and gate must verify it's specified.\n\n---\n\n## Rollout strategy \u2014 shadow mode then cutover\n\nThis is a production control loop with money burn and destructive actions. Cutover happens behind a feature flag, gated on observed divergence behavior.\n\n### Phase A \u2014 Shadow deployment (cycle ordering matters)\n\n- Reconciler ships with `shadow_mode=True` (env var: `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=shadow|authoritative`).\n- **Cycle ordering \u2014 explicit, not implicit:** at the start of each orchestrator cycle, after lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers` etc.) but **before** the legacy `_handle_early_termination` and `_calculate_scaling_decision`, capture an immutable `Observed` snapshot. Pass that frozen snapshot to the shadow reconciler. Let the legacy controller proceed with its normal logic on its own snapshot. Compare outcomes afterward.\n\n  This ordering is non-negotiable: if the shadow runs after legacy mutations, divergence data is contaminated by what legacy already did, and the audit becomes meaningless.\n\n- Shadow does `OBSERVE \u2192 PLAN \u2192 ADMIT \u2192 RECORD`. **It does NOT call `act()` in shadow mode.** Intent rows are written with `shadow=true`.\n- Existing two-controller path continues to be authoritative. Nothing user-facing changes.\n- A divergence audit query becomes the validation surface:\n  ```sql\n  -- Shadow's intended actions per cycle vs. legacy's actual outcomes per cycle, last 24h\n  SELECT\n    sl.created_at::date,\n    sl.metadata->>'pool' AS pool,\n    sl.metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' AS shadow_action,\n    -- join against actual worker-row mutations from legacy controller\n    ...\n  FROM system_logs sl\n  WHERE sl.source_type = 'orchestrator_gpu'\n    AND sl.metadata ? 'shadow_intended_action'\n    AND sl.created_at > now() - interval '24 hours';\n  ```\n  (Planner should write the full join against the `workers` table mutations or against a parallel `legacy_actions` log if one exists.)\n\n### Phase B \u2014 Cutover decision criteria\n\nCutover from shadow to authoritative requires ALL of:\n\n1. **\u226524 hours of shadow mode in production traffic AND \u2265N meaningful capacity events.** \"Meaningful event\" = a cycle where shadow_desired \u2260 legacy_action OR shadow proposed `CancelPendingSpawn` OR backoff suppressed a spawn. N = at least 50; if 24h of normal traffic does not produce 50 such events, extend shadow window until they accumulate.\n2. **Replay validation against captured production state.** Before cutover, the planner must produce a replay test that loads a captured snapshot of `workers` + `tasks` from the actual 2026-05-13 incident window (or a similar pathological window) and runs the reconciler against it. Assert: at the moment `queued` momentarily drops to 0 with active work in flight, the reconciler does NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn` for the spawning worker. This is the **non-negotiable proof of correctness**.\n3. **No divergence in scenarios where the legacy controller's behavior is correct.** If shadow says spawn while legacy correctly says terminate-idle on a genuinely-idle worker, that's a reconciler bug, not a legacy bug \u2014 fix and re-shadow.\n\n### Phase C \u2014 Cutover\n\n- Flip `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=authoritative`. Reconciler becomes authoritative; legacy controller becomes a no-op stub kept for one more day.\n- **At the moment of flag flip**, the reconciler's first OBSERVE will discover spawning workers with no prior intent row (they were spawned by legacy). Apply rule 9 above (synthetic adoption intent).\n- Watch `scaling-audit --hours 6` output for 4 hours post-cutover. Effective utilisation should rise from baseline.\n- 24h later: delete legacy stub, delete shadow-mode logging, delete the duplicate mixin code path.\n\n---\n\n## Deliverables (acceptance criteria)\n\nThe plan must produce, and execute must ship:\n\n1. **Migration:** `worker_capacity_intents` table + indexes in `sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`, registered with the orchestrator's existing migration system.\n2. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`** + supporting modules (`capacity_plan.py` for pure PLAN computation, types/dataclasses for `CapacityPlan` / `Action` / `Observed`).\n3. **`_handle_early_termination` deleted** from `control_loop.py`. Verified by grep returning zero matches.\n4. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree deleted** (and any tests that exercised it removed or rewritten against the new reconciler).\n5. **Single-writer lease/lock mechanism** for authoritative mode (advisory lock or `orchestrator_leases` table; planner picks, defends choice in plan output).\n6. **Cycle-trace simulation tests** under `tests/control/` covering:\n   - **Test A \u2014 Queued snapshot gate:** Queued drops to zero mid-render while 1 active worker has 5 in-progress segments and a spawning replacement is in flight. Reconciler must NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn`. Spawning worker survives.\n   - **Test B \u2014 Spawn backoff with fake clock:** Use a fake/frozen clock. RunPod returns `spawn_failed` 4 times in succession. Assert exact `next_spawn_allowed_at` after each failure (30s, 60s, 120s, 240s). After the clock advances past `next_spawn_allowed_at`, a fifth attempt succeeds and `consecutive_spawn_failures` resets to 0. **Required: a `Clock` injection seam** in the reconciler \u2014 no `datetime.now()` called directly inside `plan()` or `admit()`.\n   - **Test C \u2014 Time-based hysteresis on cancel:** Spawning worker has been spawning for 250s when `desired_capacity` drops from 2 to 1. Reconciler must NOT immediately cancel. Cancellation may only emit when desired \u2264 effective has held for \u2265 `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time across at least two snapshots, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Test both branches.\n   - **Test D \u2014 Route-stale workers:** A worker with `is_route_stale=True` (per `worker_state.py:83` definition based on `worker_contract_version` and route contract compatibility) is present in the worker list. Reconciler must NOT count it toward `effective_capacity` when satisfying a fresh-route `desired`.\n   - **Test E \u2014 Cutover adoption:** Pre-existing spawning worker rows with no intent-table predecessor are correctly adopted on first authoritative `observe()`. They are not cancelled. A synthetic adoption intent row is written.\n   - **Test F \u2014 Replay against captured incident state:** Load fixture state representing the 2026-05-13 14:11-14:14 window (or equivalent synthetic pathological state). Run reconciler. Assert no oscillation, no premature cancel.\n7. **Shadow-mode infrastructure:** `shadow_mode` flag, immutable `Observed` snapshot captured at the correct point in the cycle (before legacy capacity actions), shadow logging via `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` + metadata tag, divergence audit SQL committed to `scripts/` or `sql/`.\n8. **`scaling-audit` debug command extended** in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` to read `worker_capacity_intents` and report on shadow vs. authoritative cycles, divergence counts, and per-pool intent stability. Cross-repo touch; small and well-scoped.\n9. **Feature-flag cutover plumbing:** `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE` env var controls shadow vs. authoritative. No-op stub of legacy controller for 24h post-cutover.\n\n---\n\n## Out of scope (do not touch in this sprint)\n\n- **Cross-pool intent reasoning.** Each pool reconciled independently. Cross-pool rebalancing is a separate brief.\n- **Simplifying `min_active_gpus` / `machines_to_keep_idle` / `buffer_based`.** Stays as input to `plan()`. The plan output must *specify* the composition (precedence order), but not change the input semantics.\n- **UI / dashboard for the intent table.** `SELECT` queries are the interface.\n- **Worker-side code (`reigh-worker/`).** Untouched.\n- **App-side code (`reigh-app/`).** Only the cross-repo touch is extending `scaling-audit` debug command, which is small and well-scoped.\n- **Replacing the existing config/feature-flag system.** Use whatever's already there.\n- **Flipping production to the `control/phases/` mixin path.** Out of scope. The mixins are being deleted, not promoted.\n\n---\n\n## Watch items (gate signals)\n\nThe gate phase MUST verify each of these before allowing execute to proceed:\n\n- **W1: Concurrency on `worker_capacity_intents` writes.** Multiple orchestrator replicas could exist (Railway autoscaling). Planner must specify uniqueness strategy: `UNIQUE (pool, cycle_id)` constraint, advisory lock around the write, or accept duplicates and let queries dedupe. Gate verifies the choice is defensible.\n- **W2: Cutover sequencing.** What happens to in-flight spawning workers at the moment of `shadow_mode=False` flip? Rule 9 specifies adoption; gate verifies the implementation actually does it.\n- **W3: Duplicate code path deletion completeness.** Verify by grep that `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` is removed AND no production code path imports from it AND any test rewrites are passing.\n- **W4: Idempotency under cycle restart.** If `act()` partially completes (spawned one of two pods, then process crashes), the next cycle must not double-spawn. Rule 7 addresses this; gate verifies the implementation.\n- **W5: Test fidelity.** Test F's captured-state fixture must be realistic. Planner should examine actual `workers` + `tasks` snapshots from the incident window if available, or synthetic state that reproduces the failure mode.\n- **W6: Single-writer semantics.** Verify the advisory lock or lease implementation prevents two Railway replicas from acting on the same pool's intent simultaneously. Test by spawning two reconciler instances in the test harness against the same DB.\n- **W7: Migration registration.** Verify the new migration in `sql/` is wired into whatever runner the orchestrator uses (likely a script invoked at Railway deploy time or referenced from `railway.json`). Migration must be idempotent.\n- **W8: Cutover adoption test passes.** Test E above must pass before any cutover plan is signed off. Pre-existing spawning workers must not be orphaned or cancelled.\n\n---\n\n## Risk: what would falsify the architectural premise\n\nIf during prep or plan the planner discovers that the \"two formulas computing different `desired`\" diagnosis is wrong \u2014 e.g., the early-term and scale-up phases actually coordinate correctly through some mechanism this brief overlooked \u2014 **stop and report**. Do not proceed with deleting `_handle_early_termination` based on a faulty premise.\n\nLess drastically: if the planner finds that the `CapacityPlan` abstraction can't cleanly express some real-world scenario in the existing `worker_state.py` decision logic (around lines 588-780), surface that during critique. The right answer might be a different decomposition than OBSERVE/PLAN/ADMIT/ACT/RECORD.\n\nThe whole point of running this at `high` author depth is so the planner can push back on the brief's design if it's wrong.\n\n---\n\n## Reference materials in the repo\n\n- `docs/scaling-churn-postmortem-20260513.md` \u2014 symptom-level postmortem.\n- `docs/scaling-churn-approach-20260513.md` \u2014 approach memo that this brief is the formal version of.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` \u2014 file being substantially rewritten. Key lines: `:340` (early-term entry), `:362` (current_capacity derivation), `:377` (queued > 0 gate), `:802` (scaling decision call).\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py` \u2014 `:83` (`is_route_stale` definition), `:588` (queued coverage spawn math), `:594` (busy-worker buffer floor), `:616-780` (legacy `desired_workers` block and existing decision logic).\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` \u2014 test-only mixin tree being deleted.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/` \u2014 migration directory; existing files use `YYYYMMDDhhmmss_descriptive_name.sql` pattern.\n- `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` \u2014 `scaling-audit` command lives here; extend to read the new table.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/railway.json` \u2014 Railway deploy config; check for migration runner wiring.\n\nPrevious successful rollout for reference pattern: `brief-stop-the-cross-boundary-20260513-1358` (today, merged to main as commits `b1675676c`, `04fd9d759`, `d813238a3` in reigh-app; `3bfe7ac9` in reigh-worker; `fcec14f` in reigh-worker-orchestrator).",
