        "id": "FLAG-001",
        "concern": "Single-writer: pg_try_advisory_xact_lock cannot work as written against PostgREST/Supabase. Advisory locks are transaction-scoped, but each supabase.rpc() / supabase.table().execute() call is its own HTTP request and its own transaction. Two Railway replicas will acquire+release the lock between independent statements and proceed in lockstep. Plan must specify either (a) an RPC function that owns the full admit+act transaction, or (b) a leases-table mechanism with conditional UPDATE inside an RPC.",
        "resolution": "v2 addresses 20 significant flags from the gate, clustered on five concrete defects. (1) Single-writer: replaced PostgREST-incompatible advisory locks with a Postgres RPC `func_reconcile_capacity` that owns lease + state-mutation + intent-INSERT in one transaction, backed by a new `orchestrator_leases` table. (2) Storage split: `worker_capacity_intents` stays append-only audit with `cycle_uuid` (no `UNIQUE(pool, cycle_id)`); mutable backoff moves into a new `worker_capacity_state` table keyed on `(pool, route_key)`. (3) Backoff trigger: `consecutive_spawn_failures` is now derived in OBSERVE from the `workers` table over a configurable lookback window, covering all spawning-phase termination reasons (`gpu_not_detected`, `ready_not_claiming_timeout`, etc.), not just synchronous `spawn_failed` returns; same derivation in shadow and authoritative so suppression events are observable pre-cutover. (4) Spawn primitive: extracted `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` into a free `spawn_one_worker` function; the reconciler calls this same primitive (no `runpod.create_pod` invention). (5) Legacy `desired` removal: `_execute_scaling`'s idle-timeout sizing block now consumes `self._last_reconciliation.plan.desired` in authoritative mode, so the legacy formula stops being authoritative anywhere. Also added: Railway `releaseCommand` for migration application, direct-INSERT path for shadow logs (bypasses logger that nests extras under `metadata.extra`), real-incident-fixture capture script (synthetic only as documented fallback), audit of `setup_database.py`/`show_migrations.py` for migration enumeration, new Tests G/H/I/J for single-writer/idempotency/audit-key/lifecycle-backoff. Cross-repo `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` touch demoted to a separate ticket; in-repo `scripts/scaling_audit_cli.py` replaces it for Phase B validation."

(1) Single-writer: replaced PostgREST-incompatible advisory locks with a Postgres RPC `func_reconcile_capacity` that owns lease + state-mutation + intent-INSERT in one transaction, backed by a new `orchestrator_leases` table. (2) Storage split: `worker_capacity_intents` stays append-only audit with `cycle_uuid` (no `UNIQUE(pool, cycle_id)`); mutable backoff moves into a new `worker_capacity_state` table keyed on `(pool, route_key)`. (3) Backoff trigger: `consecutive_spawn_failures` is now derived in OBSERVE from the `workers` table over a configurable lookback window, covering all spawning-phase termination reasons (`gpu_not_detected`, `ready_not_claiming_timeout`, etc.), not just synchronous `spawn_failed` returns; same derivation in shadow and authoritative so suppression events are observable pre-cutover. (4) Spawn primitive: extracted `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` into a free `spawn_one_worker` function; the reconciler calls this same primitive (no `runpod.create_pod` invention). (5) Legacy `desired` removal: `_execute_scaling`'s idle-timeout sizing block now consumes `self._last_reconciliation.plan.desired` in authoritative mode, so the legacy formula stops being authoritative anywhere. Also added: Railway `releaseCommand` for migration application, direct-INSERT path for shadow logs (bypasses logger that nests extras under `metadata.extra`), real-incident-fixture capture script (synthetic only as documented fallback), audit of `setup_database.py`/`show_migrations.py` for migration enumeration, new Tests G/H/I/J for single-writer/idempotency/audit-key/lifecycle-backoff. Cross-repo `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` touch demoted to a separate ticket; in-repo `scripts/scaling_audit_cli.py` replaces it for Phase B validation."
      },

    {
      "id": "correctness-1",
      "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: ON CONFLICT semantics in Step 1.4 lease acquisition: 'INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (scope_kind, scope_key) DO UPDATE SET ... WHERE expires_at < now() OR holder_id = EXCLUDED.holder_id RETURNING holder_id = $caller'. When the row exists and WHERE evaluates false, Postgres performs no INSERT and no UPDATE, so RETURNING produces zero rows. The caller must interpret 'zero rows returned' as lease-not-acquired \u2014 not 'no error therefore success'. The plan doesn't spell this out; an executor who reads `INSERT ... RETURNING bool` literally will treat NULL/empty result as falsy and skip ahead. Worth calling out.",
      "category": "correctness",
      "severity_hint": "likely-significant",
      "evidence": "ON CONFLICT semantics in Step 1.4 lease acquisition: 'INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (scope_kind, scope_key) DO UPDATE SET ... RETURNING bool` literally will treat NULL/empty result as falsy and skip ahead. Worth calling out.",

Too short (e.g., 30s = one poll) means a single slow cycle releases the lease and the other replica takes over mid-spawn. Too long (e.g., 5 min) means a crashed orchestrator delays recovery. With numReplicas=1 in railway.json today the risk is low, but the spec needs a value for executors to land tests against.",

      "id": "correctness-4",
      "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Backoff reset condition: invariant 5 says 'Successful spawn for that (pool, route_key) resets the counter to 0.' Plan Step 5.1 defines consecutive_spawn_failures as 'count of consecutive failures since the most recent successful promotion-to-active for (pool, route_key).' That's a different signal \u2014 a worker's spawn-attempt succeeds when the pod is created (current spawn_failed surface), but 'promotion to active' is the lifecycle completion. These are minutes apart. The brief's reset condition wants reset-on-successful-pod-creation; the plan's wants reset-on-promotion-to-active, which delays reset and overcounts failures during the initialization window. Probably the planner's choice is better (more conservative), but the deviation from the brief should be acknowledged.",

(2) Storage split: `worker_capacity_intents` stays append-only audit with `cycle_uuid` (no `UNIQUE(pool, cycle_id)`); mutable backoff moves into a new `worker_capacity_state` table keyed on `(pool, route_key)`. (4) Spawn primitive: extracted `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` into a free `spawn_one_worker` function; the reconciler calls this same primitive (no `runpod.create_pod` invention). (5) Legacy `desired` removal: `_execute_scaling`'s idle-timeout sizing block now consumes `self._last_reconciliation.plan.desired` in authoritative mode, so the legacy formula stops being authoritative anywhere.     "id": "correctness-1",
    "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: ON CONFLICT semantics in Step 1.4 lease acquisition: 'INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (scope_kind, scope_key) DO UPDATE SET ... WHERE expires_at < now() OR holder_id = EXCLUDED.holder_id RETURNING holder_id = $caller'. When the row exists and WHERE evaluates false, Postgres performs no INSERT and no UPDATE, so RETURNING produces zero rows. The caller must interpret 'zero rows returned' as lease-not-acquired \u2014 not 'no error therefore success'. The plan doesn't spell this out; an executor who reads `INSERT ... RETURNING bool` literally will treat NULL/empty result as falsy and skip ahead. Worth calling out.",
    "evidence": "ON CONFLICT semantics in Step 1.4 lease acquisition: 'INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (scope_kind, scope_key) DO UPDATE SET ... WHERE expires_at < now() OR holder_id = EXCLUDED.holder_id RETURNING holder_id = $caller'. RETURNING bool` literally will treat NULL/empty result as falsy and skip ahead. Worth calling out.",

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

> AGENT

Looking at iteration 2: 9 v1 flags resolved, score moved 29.5→27.5, plan delta 92%. But the v2 critique surfaced concrete *new* problems that the planner couldn't have anticipated without deeper inspection — most notably that `scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` is itself broken (calls non-existent `supabase.rpc('sql', ...)`, silently exits 0) and the GPU orchestrator Dockerfile build context excludes `scripts/` and `sql/` entirely. Those make the v2 deploy story non-functional. The remaining flags (lease TTL, ON CONFLICT RETURNING semantics, backoff cache-vs-derived precedence, CycleSummary integration, RLS pattern) are concrete plan-quality gaps, not architectural tensions. No flag has been addressed-then-reopened. Recommendation is ITERATE.

        Idea:
# brief-unify-capacity-control-20260513

**Profile:** `all-codex/standard/high`
**Repo:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator` (Python, Railway-deployed) + small cross-repo touch in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py`
**Branch base:** `origin/main` at `fcec14f`
**Rollout:** shadow-mode → divergence audit → flag-flip cutover

> **Brief revision history:** drafted 2026-05-13. Sense-checked by Codex GPT-5.5 `high` (read-only, repo-aware). Corrections from that review folded in below — most importantly: code-level claims about `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` and the cycle phase order have been corrected against ground truth.

---

## Goal

Replace the orchestrator's two-controller scaling loop with one durable-intent reconciler. Specifically:

1. Introduce `worker_capacity_intents` (Postgres) as the durable source of truth for "what capacity should exist right now, for what reason, with what cooldowns."
2. Build `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py` with an explicit five-phase state machine: **OBSERVE → PLAN → ADMIT → ACT → RECORD**.
3. Delete `_handle_early_termination` entirely. Its job becomes a `cancel_pending_spawn` action emitted by the reconciler under strictly-defined conditions.
4. Fold RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff into the same reconciler as admission control (no separate guardrail path).
5. Delete the test-only duplicate code path under `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` (which is *not* the production runtime path — see "Context" below).
6. Roll out via shadow mode: new reconciler runs alongside the existing controller, logs intended actions, doesn't take effect until a feature flag flips.

Success is measured by **production effective-utilisation rising from ~4% to ≥70%** sustained over 24h post-cutover, with no user-task failures attributable to the new controller.

---

## Context — what's broken

A 2-hour rollout window on 2026-05-13 observed **73 GPU workers spawned, 70 killed before becoming productive, 1 worker did all the actual work** (4% effective utilisation). The same pattern is present in git history going back at least 6 weeks.

Two parallel Codex GPT-5.5 sense-checks (independent runs, no cross-talk) reading `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` converged on a single architectural diagnosis, **subsequently verified by a third Codex `high` review** against the actual code:

> The orchestrator runs **two independent capacity controllers** in one cycle. `_handle_early_termination` (around `control_loop.py:340`, `current_capacity` derived at `:362`, gate check at `:377`) computes one `desired` figure from `task_counts.total * scale_down_multiplier` + min GPUs + idle buffer. Later in the same cycle, `_calculate_scaling_decision` calls `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` at `control_loop.py:802` (when `use_new_scaling_logic` is enabled), which emits spawn action math from queued coverage (`workers_covering_tasks = idle + spawning`, `uncovered_tasks = queued - workers_covering_tasks`, `spawn_for_tasks = uncovered_tasks`, see `worker_state.py:588`) plus a busy-worker buffer floor (`worker_state.py:594`). **However**, `desired_workers` itself is still populated from the legacy `total_workload * scale_up_multiplier`, `busy + idle buffer`, and `min_active_gpus` block at `worker_state.py:616`.
>
> The bug is **not** "stale snapshot between phases" — the scaling decision is computed *after* early-term, not before. The bug is that early-term **mutates the worker list** (terminating spawning workers) before a second, separate scaling formula runs against the same phase-1 task snapshot and the now-changed worker list. Two formulas, two notions of `desired`, one cycle, opposing actions.

Three named failure modes ride on this defect:

- **Spawn churn from snapshot gates.** `_handle_early_termination` is guarded by `if task_counts.queued > 0` (at `control_loop.py:377`). `task_counts.queued` is an instantaneous snapshot taken once at the top of each cycle. When the single productive worker had just claimed a segment (queue momentarily empty, six in-progress segments active), early-term fired and killed the spawning replacement. Next cycle re-spawned. Loop.
- **No backoff for RunPod `spawn_failed`.** 55 of the 73 deaths were RunPod refusing pod allocation. The orchestrator retried at 30-second cycle cadence indefinitely.
- **Mutating actions sequenced before mismatched re-derivation.** Early-term terminates spawning workers from the worker list, then `_calculate_scaling_decision` re-derives `desired` against the same phase-1 task snapshot but a now-shorter worker list, and decides to re-spawn what early-term just killed.

A team-authored postmortem (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-postmortem-20260513.md`) and approach memo (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-approach-20260513.md`) cover the symptom-level analysis. The architectural prescription below is the *deep* fix.

### Duplicate code path — clarified

`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` is **the** production runtime path. `main.py` imports `OrchestratorControlLoop` from it. The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree contains duplicated chunks of capacity logic (including a duplicate `_handle_early_termination`) but is **only exercised by tests, not by production**. Default deletion target for this sprint: the mixin duplication under `control/phases/`. The planner may override that choice only by first showing concretely that the mixins contain logic worth migrating into `control_loop.py` (or by proposing to flip production over to the mixin path — but that is out of scope for this sprint and would be a separate brief).

---

## Architecture — what we're building

### New Postgres table

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.worker_capacity_intents (
  id                          uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  pool                        text NOT NULL,
  route_key                   text,                              -- nullable for pool-wide intents
  desired_capacity            int  NOT NULL,
  reason                      text NOT NULL,                     -- 'queued_coverage'|'min_floor'|'cooldown'|...
  observed_queued             int,
  observed_active             int,
  observed_spawning           int,
  cycle_id                    bigint,
  created_at                  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  valid_until                 timestamptz,                       -- intent expires; absence ≠ kill
  stable_since                timestamptz,                       -- when desired_capacity last changed
  consecutive_spawn_failures  int  NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  next_spawn_allowed_at       timestamptz,                       -- backoff cooldown
  shadow                      boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true      -- shadow vs authoritative mode
);

CREATE INDEX worker_capacity_intents_pool_route_created
  ON public.worker_capacity_intents (pool, route_key, created_at DESC);
```

**One row written per cycle per pool**, regardless of whether the intent changed. This is the audit trail.

**Migration location:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/` (alongside the existing `2025*_*.sql` files). Filename pattern: `20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`. The repo's existing migration registration system applies — planner should locate how it's wired (likely `scripts/` or a runner referenced from Railway config) and follow the established pattern.

### Logging — `system_logs` constraint

Existing `system_logs.valid_source_type` CHECK constraint permits only `orchestrator_gpu`, `orchestrator_api`, and `worker`. **Do not invent a new `source_type` value.** Use `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` with a metadata tag field (e.g., `metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' = 'true'`) to distinguish shadow log entries from authoritative ones. Saves a migration and avoids breaking other readers of `system_logs`.

### New Python module — `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`

Owns the full five-phase state machine. Pure-where-possible, side-effects concentrated at boundaries.

```python
class CapacityReconciler:
    def reconcile(self, cycle_id: int) -> ReconciliationResult:
        observed = self.observe()                     # phase 1: read workers + tasks
        plan = self.plan(observed)                    # phase 2: compute CapacityPlan
        admitted = self.admit(plan)                   # phase 3: filter by backoff/cooldown
        result = self.act(admitted, shadow=self.shadow_mode)  # phase 4: side effects (or log-only)
        self.record(cycle_id, observed, plan, admitted, result)  # phase 5: persist intent row
        return result
```

Where:

- **`observe()`** is the canonical read of `workers` + `tasks` state. Counts active / spawning / route-stale / idle, computes `total_tasks = queued + in_progress`. Returns a frozen `Observed` dataclass — **immutable**, taken at one moment in time, passed by value through subsequent phases.
- **`plan(observed)`** computes ONE `desired_capacity` from ONE formula. Returns a `CapacityPlan` with `desired`, `effective_capacity = active + spawning - admitted_pending_cancels`, `actions = [Spawn | CancelPendingSpawn | TerminateIdle | Noop]`, `reason`, `valid_until`.
- **`admit(plan)`** is admission control. Checks `next_spawn_allowed_at` per pool, drops `Spawn` actions during cooldown, logs `suppressed_by_backoff`.
- **`act(admitted, shadow)`** executes side effects when `shadow=False`. When `shadow=True`, logs intended actions to `system_logs` (source_type=`orchestrator_gpu`, metadata tag `shadow_intended_action`). **In shadow mode, `act()` MUST NOT call RunPod, MUST NOT mutate worker rows, MUST NOT update backoff state. Shadow is read-only on the external world.**
- **`record(...)`** writes one row to `worker_capacity_intents` regardless of mode. The `shadow` boolean column distinguishes shadow rows from authoritative rows.

### Rules the reconciler MUST honor (invariants)

These are invariants, not preferences. The cycle-trace simulation tests in the deliverables section exist to enforce them.

1. **Spawning workers count as capacity** until they (a) report `spawn_failed`, (b) exceed `active_initializing_max_sec`, or (c) are explicitly cancelled by a `CancelPendingSpawn` action emitted by this reconciler.
2. **Never kill or cancel a spawning worker because `queued == 0`.** The queue snapshot does not have authority over an authorized spawn.
3. **Cancel `Spawn` action emission** only when the desired_capacity has remained ≤ effective for **at least two consecutive observed reconciler snapshots spanning at least `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time**, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Wall-clock based, not iteration-count based — duplicate Railway replicas or delayed cycles make raw cycle count unreliable.
4. **Scale down active workers** only via idle timeout + min/buffer constraints. Active workers are never killed by capacity-pressure logic.
5. **RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff is keyed by `(pool, route_key)`**, not globally. `consecutive_spawn_failures += 1` and `next_spawn_allowed_at = now() + min(2^N * 30s, 600s)`. Successful spawn for that `(pool, route_key)` resets the counter to 0.
6. **`record()` writes exactly one row per pool per cycle**, even when nothing changed. Audit trail integrity.
7. **No authoritative spawn action executes without a prior `RECORD` write succeeding** within the same reconciliation. This means `act(shadow=False)` calls `record()` first and only then dispatches RunPod creates / cancellations. Idempotency: if `act()` partially completes and the process crashes, the next cycle sees the recorded intent and can resume.
8. **Single-writer per pool per cycle in authoritative mode.** Use a Postgres advisory lock keyed on `hashtext(pool)` or a lease row in a small `orchestrator_leases` table, held for the duration of `admit()` + `act()`. Without this, two Railway replicas can each spawn against the same pool's intent and double-provision. (Shadow mode does not need the lock — multiple shadows are fine since they don't mutate.)
9. **At cutover, the reconciler must adopt pre-existing spawning workers** that have no associated intent row. These were spawned by the legacy controller before cutover. Treat them as part of effective_capacity; assign a synthetic adoption intent on first observe, do not cancel them.

### Phasing — what gets deleted, what survives

**Deleted:**
- `_handle_early_termination` (the entire phase 2 block in `control_loop.py` at lines ~340-400).
- The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree (test-only duplicate code path). Includes any tests that exercised it; replace those with tests against the new reconciler.

**Survives:**
- Worker lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers`, heartbeat checks, promote-to-active logic) — stays in `control_loop.py`.
- RunPod pod creation primitives (`runpod_client.create_pod` and friends) — stay. `act()` calls into them.
- `min_active_gpus`, `machines_to_keep_idle`, `buffer_based` calculations — stay as **inputs** to `plan()`. **Out of scope for this sprint** is simplifying or renaming them; `plan()` consumes them and emits one authoritative `desired` from one formula. The planner MUST write down exactly that composition (what's the precedence order between `min_active_gpus` floor, `queued_coverage`, and `busy_buffer`?) in the plan output, and gate must verify it's specified.

---

## Rollout strategy — shadow mode then cutover

This is a production control loop with money burn and destructive actions. Cutover happens behind a feature flag, gated on observed divergence behavior.

### Phase A — Shadow deployment (cycle ordering matters)

- Reconciler ships with `shadow_mode=True` (env var: `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=shadow|authoritative`).
- **Cycle ordering — explicit, not implicit:** at the start of each orchestrator cycle, after lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers` etc.) but **before** the legacy `_handle_early_termination` and `_calculate_scaling_decision`, capture an immutable `Observed` snapshot. Pass that frozen snapshot to the shadow reconciler. Let the legacy controller proceed with its normal logic on its own snapshot. Compare outcomes afterward.

  This ordering is non-negotiable: if the shadow runs after legacy mutations, divergence data is contaminated by what legacy already did, and the audit becomes meaningless.

- Shadow does `OBSERVE → PLAN → ADMIT → RECORD`. **It does NOT call `act()` in shadow mode.** Intent rows are written with `shadow=true`.
- Existing two-controller path continues to be authoritative. Nothing user-facing changes.
- A divergence audit query becomes the validation surface:
  ```sql
  -- Shadow's intended actions per cycle vs. legacy's actual outcomes per cycle, last 24h
  SELECT
    sl.created_at::date,
    sl.metadata->>'pool' AS pool,
    sl.metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' AS shadow_action,
    -- join against actual worker-row mutations from legacy controller
    ...
  FROM system_logs sl
  WHERE sl.source_type = 'orchestrator_gpu'
    AND sl.metadata ? 'shadow_intended_action'
    AND sl.created_at > now() - interval '24 hours';
  ```
  (Planner should write the full join against the `workers` table mutations or against a parallel `legacy_actions` log if one exists.)

### Phase B — Cutover decision criteria

Cutover from shadow to authoritative requires ALL of:

1. **≥24 hours of shadow mode in production traffic AND ≥N meaningful capacity events.** "Meaningful event" = a cycle where shadow_desired ≠ legacy_action OR shadow proposed `CancelPendingSpawn` OR backoff suppressed a spawn. N = at least 50; if 24h of normal traffic does not produce 50 such events, extend shadow window until they accumulate.
2. **Replay validation against captured production state.** Before cutover, the planner must produce a replay test that loads a captured snapshot of `workers` + `tasks` from the actual 2026-05-13 incident window (or a similar pathological window) and runs the reconciler against it. Assert: at the moment `queued` momentarily drops to 0 with active work in flight, the reconciler does NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn` for the spawning worker. This is the **non-negotiable proof of correctness**.
3. **No divergence in scenarios where the legacy controller's behavior is correct.** If shadow says spawn while legacy correctly says terminate-idle on a genuinely-idle worker, that's a reconciler bug, not a legacy bug — fix and re-shadow.

### Phase C — Cutover

- Flip `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=authoritative`. Reconciler becomes authoritative; legacy controller becomes a no-op stub kept for one more day.
- **At the moment of flag flip**, the reconciler's first OBSERVE will discover spawning workers with no prior intent row (they were spawned by legacy). Apply rule 9 above (synthetic adoption intent).
- Watch `scaling-audit --hours 6` output for 4 hours post-cutover. Effective utilisation should rise from baseline.
- 24h later: delete legacy stub, delete shadow-mode logging, delete the duplicate mixin code path.

---

## Deliverables (acceptance criteria)

The plan must produce, and execute must ship:

1. **Migration:** `worker_capacity_intents` table + indexes in `sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`, registered with the orchestrator's existing migration system.
2. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`** + supporting modules (`capacity_plan.py` for pure PLAN computation, types/dataclasses for `CapacityPlan` / `Action` / `Observed`).
3. **`_handle_early_termination` deleted** from `control_loop.py`. Verified by grep returning zero matches.
4. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree deleted** (and any tests that exercised it removed or rewritten against the new reconciler).
5. **Single-writer lease/lock mechanism** for authoritative mode (advisory lock or `orchestrator_leases` table; planner picks, defends choice in plan output).
6. **Cycle-trace simulation tests** under `tests/control/` covering:
   - **Test A — Queued snapshot gate:** Queued drops to zero mid-render while 1 active worker has 5 in-progress segments and a spawning replacement is in flight. Reconciler must NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn`. Spawning worker survives.
   - **Test B — Spawn backoff with fake clock:** Use a fake/frozen clock. RunPod returns `spawn_failed` 4 times in succession. Assert exact `next_spawn_allowed_at` after each failure (30s, 60s, 120s, 240s). After the clock advances past `next_spawn_allowed_at`, a fifth attempt succeeds and `consecutive_spawn_failures` resets to 0. **Required: a `Clock` injection seam** in the reconciler — no `datetime.now()` called directly inside `plan()` or `admit()`.
   - **Test C — Time-based hysteresis on cancel:** Spawning worker has been spawning for 250s when `desired_capacity` drops from 2 to 1. Reconciler must NOT immediately cancel. Cancellation may only emit when desired ≤ effective has held for ≥ `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time across at least two snapshots, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Test both branches.
   - **Test D — Route-stale workers:** A worker with `is_route_stale=True` (per `worker_state.py:83` definition based on `worker_contract_version` and route contract compatibility) is present in the worker list. Reconciler must NOT count it toward `effective_capacity` when satisfying a fresh-route `desired`.
   - **Test E — Cutover adoption:** Pre-existing spawning worker rows with no intent-table predecessor are correctly adopted on first authoritative `observe()`. They are not cancelled. A synthetic adoption intent row is written.
   - **Test F — Replay against captured incident state:** Load fixture state representing the 2026-05-13 14:11-14:14 window (or equivalent synthetic pathological state). Run reconciler. Assert no oscillation, no premature cancel.
7. **Shadow-mode infrastructure:** `shadow_mode` flag, immutable `Observed` snapshot captured at the correct point in the cycle (before legacy capacity actions), shadow logging via `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` + metadata tag, divergence audit SQL committed to `scripts/` or `sql/`.
8. **`scaling-audit` debug command extended** in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` to read `worker_capacity_intents` and report on shadow vs. authoritative cycles, divergence counts, and per-pool intent stability. Cross-repo touch; small and well-scoped.
9. **Feature-flag cutover plumbing:** `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE` env var controls shadow vs. authoritative. No-op stub of legacy controller for 24h post-cutover.

---

## Out of scope (do not touch in this sprint)

- **Cross-pool intent reasoning.** Each pool reconciled independently. Cross-pool rebalancing is a separate brief.
- **Simplifying `min_active_gpus` / `machines_to_keep_idle` / `buffer_based`.** Stays as input to `plan()`. The plan output must *specify* the composition (precedence order), but not change the input semantics.
- **UI / dashboard for the intent table.** `SELECT` queries are the interface.
- **Worker-side code (`reigh-worker/`).** Untouched.
- **App-side code (`reigh-app/`).** Only the cross-repo touch is extending `scaling-audit` debug command, which is small and well-scoped.
- **Replacing the existing config/feature-flag system.** Use whatever's already there.
- **Flipping production to the `control/phases/` mixin path.** Out of scope. The mixins are being deleted, not promoted.

---

## Watch items (gate signals)

The gate phase MUST verify each of these before allowing execute to proceed:

- **W1: Concurrency on `worker_capacity_intents` writes.** Multiple orchestrator replicas could exist (Railway autoscaling). Planner must specify uniqueness strategy: `UNIQUE (pool, cycle_id)` constraint, advisory lock around the write, or accept duplicates and let queries dedupe. Gate verifies the choice is defensible.
- **W2: Cutover sequencing.** What happens to in-flight spawning workers at the moment of `shadow_mode=False` flip? Rule 9 specifies adoption; gate verifies the implementation actually does it.
- **W3: Duplicate code path deletion completeness.** Verify by grep that `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` is removed AND no production code path imports from it AND any test rewrites are passing.
- **W4: Idempotency under cycle restart.** If `act()` partially completes (spawned one of two pods, then process crashes), the next cycle must not double-spawn. Rule 7 addresses this; gate verifies the implementation.
- **W5: Test fidelity.** Test F's captured-state fixture must be realistic. Planner should examine actual `workers` + `tasks` snapshots from the incident window if available, or synthetic state that reproduces the failure mode.
- **W6: Single-writer semantics.** Verify the advisory lock or lease implementation prevents two Railway replicas from acting on the same pool's intent simultaneously. Test by spawning two reconciler instances in the test harness against the same DB.
- **W7: Migration registration.** Verify the new migration in `sql/` is wired into whatever runner the orchestrator uses (likely a script invoked at Railway deploy time or referenced from `railway.json`). Migration must be idempotent.
- **W8: Cutover adoption test passes.** Test E above must pass before any cutover plan is signed off. Pre-existing spawning workers must not be orphaned or cancelled.

---

## Risk: what would falsify the architectural premise

If during prep or plan the planner discovers that the "two formulas computing different `desired`" diagnosis is wrong — e.g., the early-term and scale-up phases actually coordinate correctly through some mechanism this brief overlooked — **stop and report**. Do not proceed with deleting `_handle_early_termination` based on a faulty premise.

Less drastically: if the planner finds that the `CapacityPlan` abstraction can't cleanly express some real-world scenario in the existing `worker_state.py` decision logic (around lines 588-780), surface that during critique. The right answer might be a different decomposition than OBSERVE/PLAN/ADMIT/ACT/RECORD.

The whole point of running this at `high` author depth is so the planner can push back on the brief's design if it's wrong.

- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py` — `:83` (`is_route_stale` definition), `:588` (queued coverage spawn math), `:594` (busy-worker buffer floor), `:616-780` (legacy `desired_workers` block and existing decision logic).
- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` — test-only mixin tree being deleted.

Previous successful rollout for reference pattern: `brief-stop-the-cross-boundary-20260513-1358` (today, merged to main as commits `b1675676c`, `04fd9d759`, `d813238a3` in reigh-app; `3bfe7ac9` in reigh-worker; `fcec14f` in reigh-worker-orchestrator).

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Unify Capacity Control via Durable-Intent Reconciler (v3)

## Overview

The orchestrator runs two parallel capacity controllers per cycle (`_handle_early_termination` at `control_loop.py:343–427` and `_calculate_scaling_decision` → `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` at `worker_state.py:556–676`) that emit opposing actions against the same phase-1 snapshot. This produces the 4 %‑utilisation churn observed on 2026‑05‑13.

The brief's "one durable-intent reconciler" is the right architecture. v1 and v2 surfaced concrete defects; v3 closes them. The work-streams below are exactly the v2 plan with the gate's nine remaining gaps closed:

1. **Migration deploy plumbing actually executes DDL.** Today `scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` calls `supabase.rpc('sql', {...})` — a non-existent RPC; failures silently exit 0. And the GPU orchestrator Docker image (`gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile`) builds against `gpu_orchestrator/` as its context, so repo-root `scripts/` and `sql/` aren't in the image. v3 rewrites the runner to use a direct Postgres connection (Supabase pooler `DATABASE_URL`) via `psycopg`, changes Railway's build context to the repo root, and adds COPY directives + a real `releaseCommand`.
2. **Lease acquisition returns a stable boolean.** The lease check moves entirely inside the PL/pgSQL RPC so the Python caller never has to interpret zero-vs-one rows from a conditional `RETURNING`.
3. **Lease TTL is concrete.** `3 * orchestrator_poll_sec` (default 90 s).
4. **Backoff source-of-truth is unambiguous.** The `workers` table is canonical; `worker_capacity_state` is a cache for `next_spawn_allowed_at` and `last_spawn_failure_*` only. `consecutive_spawn_failures` is recomputed every cycle from OBSERVE.
5. **Backoff reset condition is documented as a deviation from the brief.** Brief says "reset on successful spawn"; the plan resets on "successful promotion-to-active" because pod-creation success can still degrade into `gpu_not_detected` / `ready_not_claiming_timeout` minutes later. More conservative; explicitly acknowledged.
6. **Cache-invalidation-under-restart is testable.** New Test K simulates an orchestrator restart between failure and next OBSERVE.
7. **RLS policy mirrors the sibling pattern.** The existing `sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql:259–261` pattern is plain `GRANT SELECT, INSERT ON ... TO authenticated, anon, service_role;` with no RLS. v3 follows that — no invented policy.
8. **Spawn metadata field is additive.** `intent_row_id` is a brand-new key; it doesn't collide with existing keys (`runpod_id`, `termination_reason`, `ssh_details`, `pod_details`, `ram_tier`, `storage_volume`, `selected_route_metadata`) and lands in the same metadata dict assembled at `control_loop.py:1392`.
9. **`CycleSummary` integration.** The reconciler accepts a `CycleSummary` reference and increments `workers_spawned` / `workers_terminated` so `health_monitor` and structured logs don't under-report post-cutover.

Repo shape that matters (unchanged from v2):
- Production runtime: `gpu_orchestrator/main.py` → `OrchestratorControlLoop.run_single_cycle()` (`control_loop.py:89`).
- DB integration boundary: `gpu_orchestrator/database.py:138` (`DatabaseClient`, Supabase service-role over PostgREST).
- Spawn primitive: `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` (`control_loop.py:1371`); RunPod adapter at `gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py:53`.
- Logger handler nests caller extras under `metadata.extra` (`database_log_handler.py:182`) — direct INSERT remains required for top-level metadata keys.
- Mixin tree confirmed test-only.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Schema, RPC, Config, Migration Deploy

### Step 1: Migrations — split audit and mutable state (`sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`, `sql/20260514000001_create_orchestrator_leases.sql`, `sql/20260514000002_create_worker_capacity_state.sql`, `sql/20260514000003_func_reconcile_capacity.sql`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **`worker_capacity_intents`** — append-only audit. `cycle_uuid uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()`, `orchestrator_instance_id text NOT NULL`, `cycle_seq bigint NOT NULL`. No UNIQUE constraint on `(pool, cycle_id)`. Indexes: `(pool, route_key, created_at DESC)` and `(orchestrator_instance_id, cycle_seq)`.
2. **`worker_capacity_state`** — mutable cache, keyed on `(pool, route_key)`. Schema as in v2. **Documented role:** cache for `next_spawn_allowed_at` and `last_spawn_failure_*` only. `consecutive_spawn_failures` is recomputed each cycle from `workers` table OBSERVE; the column on this table is a *post-hoc reflection* of what OBSERVE saw, written by the RPC for audit/operator-visibility, never read back as authoritative. (Resolves correctness-3, scope.)
3. **`orchestrator_leases`** — `(scope_kind text, scope_key text)` PK, `holder_id text`, `acquired_at timestamptz`, `expires_at timestamptz`.
4. **`func_reconcile_capacity(p_payload jsonb) RETURNS jsonb`** — PL/pgSQL, all in one transaction. Pseudocode for the lease check (no reliance on conditional `RETURNING` returning rows):
   ```sql
   -- 1) Try to acquire/refresh the lease
   SELECT holder_id, expires_at INTO v_holder, v_expires
     FROM orchestrator_leases
     WHERE scope_kind = 'capacity_reconciler' AND scope_key = p_pool
     FOR UPDATE;
   IF NOT FOUND THEN
     INSERT INTO orchestrator_leases(scope_kind, scope_key, holder_id, expires_at)
       VALUES ('capacity_reconciler', p_pool, p_holder, now() + p_lease_ttl);
     v_acquired := true;
   ELSIF v_expires < now() OR v_holder = p_holder THEN
     UPDATE orchestrator_leases SET holder_id = p_holder, acquired_at = now(),
       expires_at = now() + p_lease_ttl
       WHERE scope_kind='capacity_reconciler' AND scope_key=p_pool;
     v_acquired := true;
   ELSE
     v_acquired := false;
   END IF;
   IF NOT v_acquired THEN
     RETURN jsonb_build_object('lease_acquired', false);
   END IF;
   -- 2) Upsert worker_capacity_state, 3) Insert worker_capacity_intents row, 4) Return action set.
   RETURN jsonb_build_object('lease_acquired', true, 'intent_row_id', v_intent_id, ...);
   ```
   The Python caller treats the response object's top-level `lease_acquired` key as the only signal — never inspects row counts. (Resolves correctness-1.)
5. **Lease TTL passed as `p_lease_ttl interval` = `3 * orchestrator_poll_sec`** (default 90 s). Configurable via `OrchestratorConfig.lease_ttl_seconds`. (Resolves correctness-2.)
6. **Permissions** mirror `sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql:259–261`: `GRANT SELECT, INSERT ON public.worker_capacity_intents TO authenticated, anon, service_role;` `GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE ON public.worker_capacity_state TO authenticated, anon, service_role;` `GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON public.orchestrator_leases TO authenticated, anon, service_role;` `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION func_reconcile_capacity TO authenticated, anon, service_role;`. **No RLS** — the rest of `sql/` doesn't use it. (Resolves all_locations-2.)
7. All four files use `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` / `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` for idempotency.

### Step 2: Replace the migration runner with a real DDL executor (`scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/requirements.txt`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Rewrite** `scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` to connect directly to Postgres via `psycopg` (v3) using the Supabase pooler URL exposed by Supabase as `SUPABASE_DB_URL` (or operator-supplied `DATABASE_URL`). New shape:
   - Reads all `*.sql` files from `sql/` in lexicographic order (no hardcoded list — auto-discovery from a `sql/` glob).
   - Creates a `_schema_migrations(filename text PRIMARY KEY, applied_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now())` ledger table on first run (idempotent).
   - For each unseen `.sql`, opens a transaction, executes the file's contents as a single statement block (NOT split-by-`;`; `psycopg.execute` accepts multi-statement DDL when `autocommit=False`), inserts into the ledger, commits. On error: roll back and **exit non-zero** with the file name and error message.
   - At completion, **exits 0 only if every discovered file is in the ledger**; otherwise non-zero. No "completed!" banner regardless of success.
2. **Add `psycopg[binary]>=3.1`** to `gpu_orchestrator/requirements.txt` (the GPU orchestrator image already runs Python 3.11; `psycopg[binary]` ships precompiled wheels).
3. **Smoke test** locally: `SUPABASE_DB_URL=postgresql://... python -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations` against an ephemeral Postgres (Docker `postgres:15`). Run twice to confirm the ledger short-circuits the second pass; assert exit 0 and the four new tables exist.
4. `scripts/show_migrations.py` and `scripts/setup_database.py` are left untouched — they're operator print-only / legacy tooling and not part of the deploy path. (Note this in a comment at the top of the rewritten runner.) (Resolves FLAG-011, issue_hints-1.)

### Step 3: Restructure Docker build context so `sql/` and `scripts/` are in the image (`gpu_orchestrator/railway.json`, `gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`gpu_orchestrator/railway.json`** — change `build`:
   ```json
   {
     "build": {
       "builder": "DOCKERFILE",
       "dockerfilePath": "gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile",
       "dockerContext": "."
     },
     "deploy": {
       "numReplicas": 1,
       "sleepApplication": false,
       "restartPolicyType": "ON_FAILURE",
       "restartPolicyMaxRetries": 10,
       "startCommand": "python -m gpu_orchestrator.main continuous",
       "releaseCommand": "python -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations"
     }
   }
   ```
   `dockerContext: "."` (root of the repo) plus an absolute `dockerfilePath` makes scripts/ and sql/ visible at build time. (Resolves FLAG-012, issue_hints-2, all_locations-1.)
2. **`gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile`** — update the COPY directives to reflect the new context:
   ```dockerfile
   WORKDIR /app
   COPY gpu_orchestrator/requirements.txt /tmp/requirements.txt
   RUN pip install --no-cache-dir --upgrade pip
   RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r /tmp/requirements.txt
   COPY gpu_orchestrator/ /app/gpu_orchestrator/
   COPY scripts/ /app/scripts/
   COPY sql/ /app/sql/
   COPY orchestrator_common/ /app/orchestrator_common/
   COPY runpod_lifecycle/ /app/runpod_lifecycle/
   ```
   `PYTHONPATH=/app` already (line 6) so `python -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations` resolves.
3. **Smoke test** the image: `docker build -f gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile .` from the repo root; `docker run --rm --entrypoint python <img> -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations --help` should exit 0 (i.e. the module loads).
4. Confirm the api orchestrator's separate image (`api_orchestrator/Dockerfile`, configured by repo-root `railway.json`) is unaffected — that service builds from a different `dockerfilePath` and doesn't apply migrations. (No edits to `api_orchestrator/` or the repo-root `railway.json`.)

### Step 4: Extend `OrchestratorConfig` (`gpu_orchestrator/config.py:43`)
**Scope:** Small

1. Add to `OrchestratorConfig` + `from_env()`:
   - `capacity_reconciler_mode: str` (env `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE`, default `"off"`).
   - `spawn_failure_backoff_base_sec: int` (default 30), `spawn_failure_backoff_max_sec: int` (default 600).
   - `spawn_failure_lookback_minutes: int` (default 15).
   - `cancel_hysteresis_min_seconds: int` (default `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec`).
   - `lease_ttl_seconds: int` (default `3 * orchestrator_poll_sec` = 90 s when poll=30 s).
   - `orchestrator_instance_id: str` — `os.getenv("ORCHESTRATOR_INSTANCE_ID") or f"{socket.gethostname()}/{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"` (stable per process).
2. Log all new fields in `log_config()` (line 214).

### Step 5: Reconciler types (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_types.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. Frozen dataclasses: `Observed`, `Action` (`Spawn | CancelPendingSpawn | Noop`), `BackoffState`, `CapacityPlan`, `ReconciliationResult` (includes `lease_acquired: bool`).
2. `Clock` protocol (`now() -> datetime`). `plan()` and `admit()` MUST NOT call `datetime.now()` directly.
3. `route_key = f"{worker_backend}:{worker_profile}:{selector_namespace}:{selector_version or 'default'}:v{worker_contract_version}"`.

---

## Phase 2: Reconciler Implementation

### Step 6: Pure PLAN computation (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_plan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. `compute_plan(observed, prior_intent, backoff, config, clock) -> CapacityPlan` — pure, no I/O, no `datetime.now()`.
2. **Single `desired` formula** documented as a top-of-file comment block. Formula identical to v2 (queued coverage → floor → min_gap → max cap, stale-route workers excluded from fresh `desired`'s `current_capacity` and `effective_capacity`).
3. **Cancel semantics** (invariants 2, 3): never if `queued > 0`; otherwise both (a) genuine overshoot, (b) hysteresis elapsed, (c) two-snapshot proof OR `valid_until` elapsed.
4. `valid_until = clock.now() + 2 * config.orchestrator_poll_sec`.

### Step 7: Reconciler service (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **`observe(pool, route_key)`** — async. Reads `workers` + task counts. Computes `BackoffState` **from the `workers` table** by:
   - Loading workers in the lookback window whose final `termination_reason` is in the canonical spawning-failure set (sourced from grep of `control_loop.py`): `{'spawn_failed', 'gpu_not_detected', 'ready_not_claiming_timeout', 'startup_timeout', 'spawning_timeout', 'No Runpod ID'}` plus any reason matching `STALE_ROUTE_CONTRACT*`. The set is one constant in `capacity_reconciler.py` so future additions are one-line.
   - Loading workers in the lookback window who reached `lifecycle in {ACTIVE_READY, ACTIVE_INITIALIZING}` for this `(pool, route_key)` — the most recent such timestamp is the reset point.
   - `consecutive_spawn_failures` = count of failures after that reset point. `next_spawn_allowed_at` = `max(workers_capacity_state.next_spawn_allowed_at, last_failure_at + 2^N * 30s)` capped at 600 s. The workers table is canonical; the cache row supplements only if the failure history is empty (e.g., right after a retention purge). (Resolves correctness-3, correctness-6.)
   - **Deviation note:** `lifecycle in {ACTIVE_*}` is "promotion-to-active", not pod-creation. Brief invariant 5 says "successful spawn" resets. This plan deliberately uses the stricter signal because pod-creation success regularly degrades into `gpu_not_detected` / `ready_not_claiming_timeout` minutes later, and the brief's intent is to back off until *useful* capacity comes online. Acknowledged deviation, documented in code. (Resolves correctness-4.)
2. **`plan(observed, backoff)`** — calls `compute_plan` (Step 6). Loads `prior_intent` via `db.get_latest_intent(pool, route_key)`.
3. **`admit(plan, backoff)`** — drops `Spawn` actions when `clock.now() < backoff.next_spawn_allowed_at`. Suppression is computed identically in shadow and authoritative mode (because the backoff state is derived from `workers`, which both modes can read).
4. **`act(admitted, observed, summary, shadow)`** — accepts the **`CycleSummary`** from the control loop. Two modes:
   - **Shadow:** for each admitted/suppressed action, direct-INSERT `system_logs` row via `db.insert_log` with metadata top-level keys (`shadow_intended_action`, `action_type`, `pool`, `route_key`, `desired`, `effective`, `suppressed_by_backoff`). Direct INSERT bypasses the logger handler that would nest extras under `metadata.extra`. **Also** direct-INSERT one `worker_capacity_intents` row per cycle with `shadow=true`. No RunPod calls, no worker-row mutations, no RPC, no lease.
   - **Authoritative:** call `db.reconcile_capacity_rpc({'pool', 'route_key', 'holder_id': config.orchestrator_instance_id, 'lease_ttl_seconds', 'desired', 'effective', 'reason', 'actions': [...], 'observed_*': ..., 'cycle_uuid', 'cycle_seq', 'orchestrator_instance_id'})`. The RPC returns `{lease_acquired: bool, intent_row_id: uuid|null, ...}`. If `lease_acquired==False`, log `single_writer_skipped` and return early. If True, dispatch side effects:
     - `Spawn(count=N)`: call `spawn_one_worker(db, runpod, config, intent_row_id=intent_row_id)` N times. **For each successful spawn, `summary.workers_spawned += 1`.** (Resolves callers-2.)
     - `CancelPendingSpawn(worker_id)`: call `runpod.terminate_worker(runpod_id)`, then `db.update_worker_status(worker_id, 'terminated', {'termination_reason': 'reconciler_cancel_pending_spawn', 'terminated_at': ..., 'intent_row_id': intent_row_id})`. **`summary.workers_terminated += 1`.** (Resolves callers-2.)
5. **`record(...)`** — no-op in authoritative mode (the RPC already wrote the intent row). In shadow mode, the direct INSERT in step 4 satisfies invariant 6.
6. **Invariant 7 idempotency:** RPC wrote intent row inside the lease+state transaction. Crash between RPC and side-effect dispatch leaves the intent recorded; next cycle's OBSERVE recomputes from `workers` table state. Spawn DB rows are created inside `spawn_one_worker` before the RunPod call (matches existing ordering at `control_loop.py:1376` → `:1379`), so a crash between DB-row insert and RunPod call produces an orphan `workers` row that `_handle_spawning_workers` (`control_loop.py:433`) marks `spawn_failed` next cycle. No double-spawn.

### Step 8: Database access (`gpu_orchestrator/database.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. New methods on `DatabaseClient`:
   - `get_latest_intent(pool, route_key) -> dict | None` — direct table read, ORDER BY `created_at DESC` LIMIT 1.
   - `get_recent_spawning_failures(pool, route_key, since) -> list[dict]` — direct table read against `workers`.
   - `get_recent_promotions_to_active(pool, route_key, since) -> list[dict]` — direct table read against `workers` for the reset signal.
   - `reconcile_capacity_rpc(payload) -> dict` — calls `func_reconcile_capacity`; returns the JSON object verbatim. Caller reads `lease_acquired` key.
   - `insert_log(entry) -> None` — direct INSERT into `system_logs`, bypassing the Python logger.
   - `insert_shadow_intent(row) -> uuid` — direct INSERT into `worker_capacity_intents` with `shadow=true`.
2. All new direct-table writes go through service-role; no RLS to negotiate.

## Phase 3: Wire Into the Control Loop

### Step 9: Capture snapshot and run reconciler (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. `__init__` (line 44): when `config.capacity_reconciler_mode != "off"`, build `self.reconciler = CapacityReconciler(self.db, self.config, self.runpod, clock=SystemClock(), spawn_one_worker=spawn_one_worker)`.
2. `run_single_cycle` (line 89): after Phase 2 (`_derive_all_worker_states`) and BEFORE Phase 3 `_handle_early_termination` (line 116) and Phase 8 `_calculate_scaling_decision`, build the frozen `Observed` snapshot. Call `await self.reconciler.reconcile(cycle_seq=self.cycle_count, observed=..., summary=summary)`. Stash `ReconciliationResult` on `self._last_reconciliation` for Step 10.
3. **Shadow mode:** legacy phases 3 and 8 continue authoritatively. Reconciler logs + records via shadow path.
4. **Authoritative mode:** skip `_handle_early_termination` and skip the spawn loop inside `_execute_scaling` (line 994 onward — `if decision.should_scale_up:` block).

### Step 10: Feed reconciler `desired` into idle-timeout sizing (`control_loop.py:978–983`)
**Scope:** Small

1. In authoritative mode, the idle-timeout sizing block consumes `self._last_reconciliation.plan.desired`:
   ```python
   reconciled = self._last_reconciliation
   if reconciled is not None and reconciled.lease_acquired:
       desired = reconciled.plan.desired
       current_cap = reconciled.plan.effective_capacity
   else:
       desired, current_cap = decision.desired_workers, decision.current_capacity
   max_by_capacity = max(0, current_cap - desired)
   ```
   `max_by_buffer` and `max_by_min_active` unchanged.
2. Comment notes `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` is still computed in shadow mode for parity and is removed in Phase D.

### Step 11: Refactor spawn primitive (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py:1371`)
**Scope:** Small

1. Extract `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` body (`control_loop.py:1371–1436`) into a free async function `spawn_one_worker(db, runpod_adapter, config, *, intent_row_id: str | None = None, queued_count: int = 0) -> bool` in `gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`.
2. `_spawn_worker` becomes a thin wrapper. The reconciler calls the free function directly. spawn_failed bookkeeping lives in one place.
3. **`intent_row_id` integration:** when present, add `metadata['intent_row_id'] = intent_row_id` to the metadata dict assembled at the equivalent of `control_loop.py:1392`. **This key is brand-new** — verified by grepping the existing metadata keys (`runpod_id`, `termination_reason`, `terminated_at`, `ssh_details`, `pod_details`, `ram_tier`, `storage_volume`, plus `_selected_route_metadata()` keys); no collision. The metadata dict is then passed to `db.update_worker_status` exactly as today. (Resolves callers-1.)

### Step 12: Delete legacy capacity logic (Phase D, separate commit in the same PR)
**Scope:** Small

1. After 24 h authoritative bake, in a separate revertible commit: delete `_handle_early_termination` (`control_loop.py:343–427`), delete `calculate_scaling_decision_pure`, delete the spawn branches in `_execute_scaling`. Confirm `grep -n '_handle_early_termination' gpu_orchestrator/` returns zero.
2. Delete `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` and the four mixin-facing tests (`tests/test_direct_control_health.py`, `tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_sprint7_staged_rollback_fixture.py`, `test_sprint10_canary_rollback.py`, `test_sprint12_staging_flip.py`) plus `tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/`. Confirm `grep -rn 'control\.phases' gpu_orchestrator/ tests/` returns zero.

---

## Phase 4: Tests, Fixture Capture, and Tooling

### Step 13: Capture real incident state for Test F (`scripts/dump_incident_fixture.py`, `tests/control/fixtures/incident_20260513_1411.json`)
**Scope:** Small

1. One-shot script: `SELECT * FROM workers WHERE updated_at BETWEEN '2026-05-13 14:11:00Z' AND '2026-05-13 14:14:00Z'` + contemporaneous `tasks` snapshot, saved as JSON.
2. Fallback in order: (a) target window above; (b) densest 3-minute `metadata->>'termination_reason' = 'early_termination_over_capacity'` bucket from `system_logs`; (c) synthetic shape, documented in the fixture file.

### Step 14: Cycle-trace simulation tests (`tests/control/test_capacity_reconciler.py`, `tests/control/test_capacity_plan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Test A** — Queued snapshot gate.
2. **Test B** — Spawn backoff with `FakeClock`: derived from `workers` history, exact `next_spawn_allowed_at` deltas 30/60/120/240 s, reset on first promotion-to-active.
3. **Test C** — Time-based hysteresis (both branches).
4. **Test D** — Route-stale workers excluded from `effective_capacity`.
5. **Test E** — Cutover adoption (pre-existing spawning workers without intent rows are not cancelled).
6. **Test F** — Replay against captured incident fixture (Step 13).
7. **Test G** — Single-writer via RPC against a real Postgres test container: two `CapacityReconciler` instances; exactly one gets `lease_acquired=True`, the other gets `False` and emits zero side effects. Verify the RPC's JSON `lease_acquired` key is the sole signal — no row-count interpretation in the Python client.
8. **Test H** — Idempotency under crash (kill between RPC return and `spawn_one_worker`; next cycle does not double-spawn).
9. **Test I** — Shadow audit-key visibility: `SELECT metadata->>'shadow_intended_action'` returns `'true'` (not nested under `metadata.extra`).
10. **Test J** — Backoff observes lifecycle failures (`gpu_not_detected`, `ready_not_claiming_timeout`, `spawn_failed`).
11. **Test K — Cache invalidation under restart** (NEW): seed `workers` with 3 failure rows; instantiate reconciler, run one cycle (records cache state); destroy reconciler instance; re-instantiate (simulating Railway restart); run another cycle and assert OBSERVE recomputes `consecutive_spawn_failures = 3` from `workers` (not from cache) — proves the workers table is canonical. Then add a 4th failure row that post-dates the previous cache `next_spawn_allowed_at`; assert the new cycle's `next_spawn_allowed_at` reflects the new failure. (Resolves scope.)
12. **Test L — CycleSummary integration** (NEW): mock `summary = CycleSummary()`; pass to `reconcile()`; assert `summary.workers_spawned` and `summary.workers_terminated` increment correctly for `Spawn` and `CancelPendingSpawn` actions in authoritative mode; assert no increment in shadow mode. (Resolves callers-2.)
13. **Test M — Migration runner round-trip** (NEW, `tests/scripts/test_apply_sql_migrations.py`): spin up `postgres:15` in a fixture; point `SUPABASE_DB_URL` at it; run `python -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations` twice; assert exit 0 both times, all four new tables exist, ledger has 4+ rows on first run, 0 new rows on second run, and an injected broken `.sql` file causes exit non-zero. (Resolves FLAG-011, issue_hints-1.)

### Step 15: Validation
**Scope:** Small

1. **Unit:** `pytest tests/control/test_capacity_plan.py -v`.
2. **Integration:** `pytest tests/control/test_capacity_reconciler.py -v` (Tests A–L).
3. **Migration runner:** `pytest tests/scripts/test_apply_sql_migrations.py -v` (Test M).
4. **Full suite:** `pytest tests/ -v` after mixin deletion.
5. **Two-replica RPC test:** local Docker Postgres + Test G.
6. **Docker image smoke:** `docker build -f gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile -t reconciler-test .` from repo root; `docker run --rm reconciler-test python -c "import scripts.apply_sql_migrations; print('ok')"` returns "ok".
7. **Shadow-mode local boot:** `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=shadow` against staging DB ~10 cycles; verify shadow intent rows, zero RunPod calls, top-level audit-key visibility.
8. **Authoritative staging cutover:** flip flag, verify adoption (Test E in vivo).
9. **Production cutover gates** (brief Phase B): ≥24 h shadow, ≥50 meaningful events, replay passes Test F.

### Step 16: Cross-repo `scaling-audit` is out of scope for THIS PR
**Scope:** None (deliberate)

1. Brief calls for extending `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py:scaling-audit`; that's another repo and not in this PR's reach.
2. **In this PR:** commit `sql/audit_capacity_intent_divergence.sql` and `scripts/scaling_audit_cli.py` for operator validation of Phase B.
3. The `reigh-app` extension becomes a follow-on ticket.

---

## Execution Order

1. **Schema + RPC + migration runner + Docker context** (Steps 1–3) — these unlock everything. Step 3 must follow Step 2 because the Dockerfile changes reference scripts/sql being in the build context, which Step 2 also needs.
2. **Config + types** (Steps 4–5).
3. **Pure PLAN + reconciler + DB methods** (Steps 6–8) — Step 6 is fully unit-testable without DB.
4. **Spawn primitive refactor** (Step 11) — must land before reconciler `act()` can call it.
5. **Control-loop wiring** (Steps 9–10).
6. **Fixture capture** (Step 13) — needs production DB access; do early so Test F lands green.
7. **Tests** (Step 14) — Tests A–M must all pass before authoritative cutover.
8. **Shadow staging dry run + authoritative staging cutover** (Step 15).
9. **Production cutover.**
10. **Legacy deletion** (Step 12) — separate commit, after 24 h authoritative bake.

## Validation Order

1. Pure PLAN tests (fastest signal, no DB).
2. Reconciler unit tests with mocked DB + FakeClock (Tests A–F, I, J, L).
3. Migration runner round-trip against ephemeral Postgres (Test M).
4. Single-writer RPC test against local Postgres (Test G).
5. Idempotency + cache-invalidation tests against local Postgres (Tests H, K).
6. Full `pytest tests/` after Phase-D mixin deletion.
7. Docker image build + smoke test from repo root.
8. Shadow-mode staging dry run, ≥24 h.
9. Replay validation against captured incident fixture (Test F in vivo).
10. Authoritative staging cutover; adoption verification.
11. Production cutover; effective-utilisation ≥70 % over 24 h.
