        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Unify Capacity Control via Durable-Intent Reconciler (v4)

## Overview

The architecture is settled across v1–v3 (DECISION-001 through DECISION-014 from the gate): one durable-intent reconciler at `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`, `OBSERVE → PLAN → ADMIT → ACT → RECORD`, single-writer via `func_reconcile_capacity` RPC + leases table, append-only audit + cache state split, backoff derived canonically from the `workers` table with reset on promotion-to-active, spawn primitive refactored into a free `spawn_one_worker(...)`, `_execute_scaling` fed by the reconciler's `desired` in authoritative mode, shadow logs direct-INSERTed for top-level metadata keys, mixin tree deleted in Phase D.

v4 closes the remaining mechanical gaps the gate surfaced in v3:

1. **Ledger bootstrap (FLAG-018, issue_hints-1).** The rewritten migration runner must NOT replay legacy non-idempotent DML (specifically `sql/20250121000001_fix_heartbeat_default.sql:11-15`'s `UPDATE workers SET last_heartbeat = NULL ...`). v4 pre-populates `_schema_migrations` with the six already-applied filenames on first run, so only the new files apply.
2. **Operator pre-flight gate (issue_hints-2, all_locations-1, all_locations-2).** Four platform-level facts the planner cannot verify from code must be confirmed by the operator BEFORE merge, surfaced as a checklist in the PR description: `SUPABASE_DB_URL` set on the GPU orchestrator Railway service; production-running `gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile` diff'd against the v4 file; Railway honours `build.dockerContext` JSON (or fall back to `.railwayignore`); DB connection role has CREATE FUNCTION + GRANT privileges (default Supabase pooler `postgres` role, NOT the service_role pooler).
3. **Pooler role spec (correctness-1).** Document that `SUPABASE_DB_URL` must point at Supabase's `postgres`-role pooler URL (the "Connection pooling" page, port 6543 or 5432, role `postgres`). Reject the `service_role` pooler URL up front by checking `SELECT current_user` at runner start.
4. **BEGIN/COMMIT guard (correctness-2).** Migration runner rejects files whose body contains top-level `BEGIN;` / `COMMIT;` (regex match excluding `DO $$ ... BEGIN ... END $$` blocks) so the per-file outer transaction can't conflict.
5. **`sql/` review gate (scope).** Add a CODEOWNERS entry for `sql/` (orchestrator team), plus a PR-template checklist item: "Any new `sql/*.sql` files? Confirm idempotent + reviewed for auto-apply impact." This is the cheapest defence against accidental DDL through merges.
6. **`reconciler_cancel_pending_spawn` exclusion (callers).** Add an explicit code comment in `capacity_reconciler.py`'s spawning-failure reason constant explaining why `reconciler_cancel_pending_spawn` is NOT in the set (it's an authorized cancel, not a failure), and add a test case to Test J that asserts this.

Score trajectory 29.5 → 27.5 → 23.0 with zero addressed-then-reopened flags; the remaining items are tactical edits, not architectural debate. The plan stays aligned with the brief's intent: one durable-intent reconciler replacing the two-controller scaling loop, shadow → authoritative cutover, ≥70% post-cutover utilisation. Nothing in v4 expands scope.

## Phase 0: Operator pre-flight (PR description, not code)

### Step 0: Pre-flight checklist (`PR description`)
**Scope:** Small (documentation only)

The PR description must include this block, with each box checked by the operator before merge. Failure on any one of these will break the first deploy:

```
## Pre-flight gates (operator must confirm before merge)

- [ ] `SUPABASE_DB_URL` env var is set on the GPU orchestrator's Railway service,
      pointing at Supabase's `postgres`-role pooler URL (NOT the `service_role`
      pooler). Verify via Railway dashboard → Variables.
- [ ] Production-running `gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile` (as deployed) has been
      diff'd against this PR's Dockerfile. If Railway's UI overrides the in-git
      Dockerfile or build context, reconcile before merging. (Verify by checking
      the most recent successful Railway deploy logs for the COPY directives.)
- [ ] Railway honours `build.dockerContext: "."` in `gpu_orchestrator/railway.json`.
      If the UI requires the context to be set there instead, set it in the UI
      AND keep the JSON field for documentation. Fallback: a repo-root
      `.railwayignore` excluding non-orchestrator paths.
- [ ] The DB role embedded in `SUPABASE_DB_URL` has `CREATE FUNCTION` + `GRANT`
      privileges. Default Supabase `postgres`-role pooler URLs do; the
      `service_role` pooler does NOT.
- [ ] A staging Railway deploy of this PR has been run end-to-end (build →
      releaseCommand → startCommand stays healthy ≥10 minutes) before promoting
      to production.
```

These gates are surfaced (not gated by code) because they're operator/platform facts; the planner cannot verify them from the working directory. The PR is not mergeable with any unchecked box. (Resolves issue_hints-2, all_locations-1, all_locations-2.)

## Phase 1: Foundation — Schema, RPC, Config, Migration Deploy

### Step 1: Migrations — split audit and mutable state (`sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`, `sql/20260514000001_create_orchestrator_leases.sql`, `sql/20260514000002_create_worker_capacity_state.sql`, `sql/20260514000003_func_reconcile_capacity.sql`)
**Scope:** Medium

Identical to v3 Step 1 (settled). Summary of the four files:

1. **`worker_capacity_intents`** — append-only audit; `cycle_uuid uuid`, `orchestrator_instance_id text`, `cycle_seq bigint`; no UNIQUE on `(pool, cycle_id)`; index on `(pool, route_key, created_at DESC)`.
2. **`worker_capacity_state`** — mutable cache keyed on `(pool, route_key)`. SQL comment documents `consecutive_spawn_failures` as a *post-hoc reflection* of OBSERVE, never read back as authoritative; authoritative cache fields are `next_spawn_allowed_at` and `last_spawn_failure_*`.
3. **`orchestrator_leases`** — `(scope_kind, scope_key)` PK; `holder_id`, `acquired_at`, `expires_at`.
4. **`func_reconcile_capacity(p_payload jsonb) RETURNS jsonb`** — PL/pgSQL, single transaction. Lease check uses explicit IF/ELSIF/ELSE (per v3 pseudocode), always returns `{lease_acquired: bool, intent_row_id: uuid|null, ...}` as a top-level key. `p_lease_ttl` parameter = `3 * orchestrator_poll_sec` (default 90 s, configurable via `OrchestratorConfig.lease_ttl_seconds`).

Permissions mirror `sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql:259-261`: plain `GRANT SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/EXECUTE TO authenticated, anon, service_role;`. No RLS.

All four files: `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` / `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` for idempotency.

### Step 2: Migration runner with ledger bootstrap (`scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/requirements.txt`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Rewrite** `scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` to connect via `psycopg` (v3) using `os.getenv("SUPABASE_DB_URL") or os.getenv("DATABASE_URL")`. If neither is set, exit non-zero with a clear "set SUPABASE_DB_URL to the postgres-role pooler URL" message. (Resolves correctness-1's role specification at runtime.)
2. **Role check.** Immediately after connecting, execute `SELECT current_user` and assert the role is NOT `service_role` and NOT `anon`. Exit non-zero with "migration runner requires the postgres-role pooler URL; got role=<user>" if the check fails. (Resolves correctness-1.)
3. **BEGIN/COMMIT guard.** Before applying each `.sql` file, scan its contents with `re.search(r'^\s*(BEGIN|COMMIT|ROLLBACK)\s*;', body, re.MULTILINE | re.IGNORECASE)`. If matched, exit non-zero with "<file> contains top-level transaction keyword; each migration must be a single statement block managed by the runner". `DO $$ ... BEGIN ... END $$` is fine — that's a `DO` block, not a top-level `BEGIN`. (Resolves correctness-2.)
4. **Ledger setup + first-run bootstrap.** On every run:
   - Create `_schema_migrations(filename text PRIMARY KEY, applied_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now())` if missing.
   - **If the ledger is empty AND `_schema_migrations`-aware columns / tables from the legacy migrations are detectable** (concretely: `SELECT to_regclass('public.system_logs') IS NOT NULL`), pre-populate the ledger with the six existing filenames:
     ```sql
     INSERT INTO _schema_migrations(filename, applied_at) VALUES
       ('20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql', now()),
       ('20250121000001_fix_heartbeat_default.sql', now()),
       ('20250202000000_add_missing_columns.sql', now()),
       ('20250202000001_create_rpc_functions_existing.sql', now()),
       ('20250202000002_create_monitoring_views_existing.sql', now()),
       ('20250202000003_add_legacy_functions.sql', now())
     ON CONFLICT (filename) DO NOTHING;
     ```
     This guarantees the first production deploy does NOT replay `sql/20250121000001_fix_heartbeat_default.sql`'s non-idempotent UPDATE. The detection check (`system_logs` existing) is the proxy for "this is an established DB". On a fresh dev DB where `system_logs` doesn't exist yet, the ledger stays empty and ALL migrations apply in order, which is correct. (Resolves FLAG-018, issue_hints-1.)
5. **Apply loop.** Auto-discover `sorted(Path('sql').glob('*.sql'))`. For each file not in the ledger: open one transaction, execute the body (multi-statement), insert ledger row, commit. On any exception, roll back and exit non-zero with the file and error. NO "completed!" banner on failure.
6. **At end:** exit 0 only if every `sql/*.sql` is in the ledger.
7. **Add `psycopg[binary]>=3.1`** to `gpu_orchestrator/requirements.txt`.
8. `scripts/show_migrations.py` and `scripts/setup_database.py` are untouched (operator print-only / legacy tooling) — note this in a top-of-file comment in the rewritten runner.

### Step 3: Restructure Docker build context (`gpu_orchestrator/railway.json`, `gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile`)
**Scope:** Small

Identical to v3 Step 3. Set `build.dockerContext: "."`, update Dockerfile COPYs to bring `gpu_orchestrator/`, `scripts/`, `sql/`, `orchestrator_common/`, `runpod_lifecycle/` into the image. Add `deploy.releaseCommand: "python -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations"`. Pre-flight (Step 0) confirms Railway honours the JSON dockerContext or the operator falls back to `.railwayignore`.

### Step 4: `sql/` review gate (`CODEOWNERS`, `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` — only if it doesn't already exist)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** a CODEOWNERS entry: `sql/ @<orchestrator-team-handle>` (the planner doesn't know the team handle; the executor uses whatever exists in the repo's existing CODEOWNERS, or creates one with `@banodoco/orchestrator` as a placeholder for the operator to fix). If CODEOWNERS doesn't exist, create `/.github/CODEOWNERS` with that single line.
2. **Add** a PR-template line (if `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` exists, append; if not, skip — don't introduce repo-wide templates as scope creep):
   ```
   - [ ] Any new `sql/*.sql` files? Confirm idempotent + reviewed for auto-apply impact at next deploy.
   ```
3. This is a process gate, not a code change. The cost of being wrong is bounded by the BEGIN/COMMIT guard (Step 2.3) and the per-file transaction in Step 2.5: a buggy migration fails its transaction and the runner exits non-zero, blocking the deploy. (Resolves scope.)

### Step 5: Extend `OrchestratorConfig` (`gpu_orchestrator/config.py:43`)
**Scope:** Small

Identical to v3 Step 4. New fields: `capacity_reconciler_mode`, `spawn_failure_backoff_base_sec` / `_max_sec`, `spawn_failure_lookback_minutes`, `cancel_hysteresis_min_seconds`, `lease_ttl_seconds`, `orchestrator_instance_id`. Log in `log_config()`.

### Step 6: Reconciler types (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_types.py`)
**Scope:** Small

Identical to v3 Step 5. Frozen dataclasses for `Observed`, `Action`, `BackoffState`, `CapacityPlan`, `ReconciliationResult`. `Clock` protocol. `route_key` derivation.

## Phase 2: Reconciler Implementation

### Step 7: Pure PLAN computation (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_plan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

Identical to v3 Step 6. `compute_plan(...)` pure function, single `desired` formula documented as top-of-file comment block, cancel semantics honouring invariants 2 and 3, `valid_until = clock.now() + 2 * orchestrator_poll_sec`.

### Step 8: Reconciler service (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

Identical to v3 Step 7, with one addition for the `reconciler_cancel_pending_spawn` reason (resolves the new `callers` flag):

1. **Spawning-failure reason set as a module-level constant** with a documented exclusion:
   ```python
   # workers.termination_reason values that count as "failed during spawn/init"
   # for backoff purposes. NOTE: 'reconciler_cancel_pending_spawn' is deliberately
   # NOT in this set — it's an authorized cancel by this very reconciler (not a
   # spawn failure), and counting it would create a feedback loop where the
   # reconciler's own cancels trigger its own backoff.
   SPAWNING_FAILURE_TERMINATION_REASONS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({
       'spawn_failed',
       'gpu_not_detected',
       'ready_not_claiming_timeout',
       'startup_timeout',
       'spawning_timeout',
       'No Runpod ID',
       # STALE_ROUTE_CONTRACT* matched separately by prefix.
   })
   ```
2. **`observe()`** — derives `BackoffState` from the `workers` table using the constant above (plus a `startswith('STALE_ROUTE_CONTRACT')` check). Reset signal: most recent `workers.lifecycle in {ACTIVE_READY, ACTIVE_INITIALIZING}` for `(pool, route_key)`. Documented deviation from brief invariant 5 (reset-on-promotion-to-active vs reset-on-pod-creation) in a code comment.
3. **`plan(observed, backoff)`** — calls `compute_plan` (Step 7). Loads `prior_intent` via `db.get_latest_intent`.
4. **`admit(plan, backoff)`** — drops `Spawn` actions when `clock.now() < backoff.next_spawn_allowed_at`. Same derivation in shadow and authoritative mode (workers table is canonical).
5. **`act(admitted, observed, summary, shadow)`** — accepts `CycleSummary` reference:
   - **Shadow:** direct-INSERT `system_logs` rows (top-level metadata keys, bypassing the logger handler that nests caller extras under `metadata.extra`) and direct-INSERT one `worker_capacity_intents` row per cycle with `shadow=true`. No RunPod calls, no worker mutations, no RPC, no lease. Does NOT increment `summary.workers_spawned` / `workers_terminated`.
   - **Authoritative:** call `db.reconcile_capacity_rpc(...)`. Read top-level `lease_acquired` key as the sole single-writer signal. If False → log `single_writer_skipped` and return. If True → dispatch per-action side effects:
     - `Spawn(count=N)`: call `spawn_one_worker(...)` N times via the free function from Step 10. Each success: `summary.workers_spawned += 1`.
     - `CancelPendingSpawn(worker_id)`: call `runpod.terminate_worker(runpod_id)` + `db.update_worker_status(worker_id, 'terminated', {'termination_reason': 'reconciler_cancel_pending_spawn', 'terminated_at': ..., 'intent_row_id': ...})`. Each success: `summary.workers_terminated += 1`.
6. **`record(...)`** — no-op in authoritative (RPC wrote the row); shadow does its own INSERT in step 5.
7. **Invariant 7 idempotency** — RPC wrote intent before the side effects; a crash leaves the intent recorded; next cycle's OBSERVE recomputes everything from the `workers` table.

### Step 9: Database access (`gpu_orchestrator/database.py`)
**Scope:** Small

Identical to v3 Step 8. New methods: `get_latest_intent`, `get_recent_spawning_failures`, `get_recent_promotions_to_active`, `reconcile_capacity_rpc`, `insert_log`, `insert_shadow_intent`. Reads run as direct-table; the RPC is the only single-writer path.

## Phase 3: Wire Into the Control Loop

### Step 10: Refactor spawn primitive (`gpu_orchestrator/worker_spawner.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py:1371`)
**Scope:** Small

Identical to v3 Step 11. Extract `_spawn_worker` body into a free `spawn_one_worker(db, runpod_adapter, config, *, intent_row_id=None, queued_count=0)` in `worker_spawner.py`. `intent_row_id` is additive (verified no collision with `runpod_id`, `termination_reason`, `terminated_at`, `ssh_details`, `pod_details`, `ram_tier`, `storage_volume`, `_selected_route_metadata()` keys).

### Step 11: Capture snapshot and run reconciler (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

Identical to v3 Step 9. Instantiate reconciler in `__init__` when mode != "off". In `run_single_cycle`, after `_derive_all_worker_states` and BEFORE `_handle_early_termination` and `_calculate_scaling_decision`, build frozen `Observed`, call `await self.reconciler.reconcile(cycle_seq=self.cycle_count, observed=..., summary=summary)`, stash result on `self._last_reconciliation`. In authoritative mode, skip `_handle_early_termination` and the spawn branch of `_execute_scaling`.

### Step 12: Feed reconciler `desired` into idle-timeout sizing (`control_loop.py:978-983`)
**Scope:** Small

Identical to v3 Step 10. In authoritative mode `max_by_capacity` consumes `self._last_reconciliation.plan.desired`; `max_by_buffer` and `max_by_min_active` unchanged. Legacy `calculate_scaling_decision_pure.desired_workers` no longer authoritatively consulted.

### Step 13: Delete legacy capacity logic (Phase D, separate commit in same PR)
**Scope:** Small

Identical to v3 Step 12. After 24h authoritative bake, delete `_handle_early_termination`, `calculate_scaling_decision_pure`, the spawn paths in `_execute_scaling`. Delete `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` and the four mixin-facing test files. Grep verifies zero matches.

**Scope:** Medium

Tests A–M from v3 Step 14, plus three v4 additions:

- **Test J update** (lifecycle backoff observation): also asserts a `workers` row with `termination_reason='reconciler_cancel_pending_spawn'` does NOT increment `consecutive_spawn_failures` (proves the constant's exclusion takes effect). (Resolves `callers`.)
- **Test M update** (migration runner round-trip):
  - First-run-against-fresh-DB scenario: ledger empty, `system_logs` table absent → applies ALL files including legacy ones (this is the dev-bootstrap path).
  - First-run-against-established-DB scenario (the production case): seed `public.system_logs` table existence first; assert ledger is pre-populated with the six legacy filenames AND `sql/20250121000001_fix_heartbeat_default.sql`'s `UPDATE workers SET last_heartbeat = NULL ...` is NOT executed. (Resolves FLAG-018.)
  - Second-run scenario: re-running is a no-op.
  - Broken-`.sql` scenario: causes exit non-zero, runner does NOT print a "completed!" banner.
  - Top-level `BEGIN;` file scenario: causes exit non-zero with the rejection message. (Resolves correctness-2.)
  - `service_role`-pooler URL scenario: connect as a role named `service_role` → exit non-zero with the role-check message. (Resolves correctness-1.)

### Step 16: Validation
**Scope:** Small

Identical to v3 Step 15. Order: pure PLAN tests → reconciler integration tests A–L → migration runner round-trip Test M → two-replica RPC Test G → idempotency / cache invalidation Tests H/K → full pytest after mixin deletion → docker build smoke → shadow staging dry run ≥24h → authoritative staging cutover → production cutover.

### Step 17: Cross-repo `scaling-audit` out of scope for this PR
**Scope:** None (deliberate)

Identical to v3 Step 16. Commit `sql/audit_capacity_intent_divergence.sql` and `scripts/scaling_audit_cli.py` in-repo for Phase B operator validation. `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` is a follow-on ticket.

---

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 0 — Operator pre-flight checklist** in PR description (no code).
2. **Phase 1 — schema + RPC + migration runner + Docker context** (Steps 1–6). Ledger bootstrap is the critical first-deploy safeguard.
3. **Phase 2 — pure PLAN + reconciler + DB methods** (Steps 7–9).
4. **Phase 3 — spawn primitive + control-loop wiring** (Steps 10–12).
5. **Phase 4 — fixture capture + tests + validation** (Steps 14–17).
6. **Legacy deletion (Step 13)** — separate commit, after 24h authoritative bake.

## Validation Order

1. Pure PLAN tests (fastest, no DB).
2. Reconciler integration tests A–L with mocked DB + FakeClock.
3. Migration runner round-trip Test M (ephemeral Postgres, ALL six new scenarios — ledger bootstrap, BEGIN/COMMIT guard, role check, broken-file, fresh DB, established DB).
4. Two-replica RPC Test G + idempotency Test H + cache-invalidation Test K against local Postgres.
5. Full `pytest tests/` after Phase-D mixin deletion.
6. Docker image build smoke from repo root.
7. Phase 0 operator pre-flight checklist — all boxes checked.
8. Shadow-mode staging dry run ≥24h, ≥50 meaningful events.
9. Replay against captured incident fixture (Test F in vivo).
10. Authoritative staging cutover; in-vivo adoption verification.
11. Production cutover; effective-utilisation ≥70% over 24h.

        Gate signals:
        {
  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 4,
    "idea": "# brief-unify-capacity-control-20260513\n\n**Profile:** `all-codex/standard/high`\n**Repo:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator` (Python, Railway-deployed) + small cross-repo touch in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py`\n**Branch base:** `origin/main` at `fcec14f`\n**Rollout:** shadow-mode \u2192 divergence audit \u2192 flag-flip cutover\n\n> **Brief revision history:** drafted 2026-05-13. Sense-checked by Codex GPT-5.5 `high` (read-only, repo-aware). Corrections from that review folded in below \u2014 most importantly: code-level claims about `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` and the cycle phase order have been corrected against ground truth.\n\n---\n\n## Goal\n\nReplace the orchestrator's two-controller scaling loop with one durable-intent reconciler. Specifically:\n\n1. Introduce `worker_capacity_intents` (Postgres) as the durable source of truth for \"what capacity should exist right now, for what reason, with what cooldowns.\"\n2. Build `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py` with an explicit five-phase state machine: **OBSERVE \u2192 PLAN \u2192 ADMIT \u2192 ACT \u2192 RECORD**.\n3. Delete `_handle_early_termination` entirely. Its job becomes a `cancel_pending_spawn` action emitted by the reconciler under strictly-defined conditions.\n4. Fold RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff into the same reconciler as admission control (no separate guardrail path).\n5. Delete the test-only duplicate code path under `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` (which is *not* the production runtime path \u2014 see \"Context\" below).\n6. Roll out via shadow mode: new reconciler runs alongside the existing controller, logs intended actions, doesn't take effect until a feature flag flips.\n\nSuccess is measured by **production effective-utilisation rising from ~4% to \u226570%** sustained over 24h post-cutover, with no user-task failures attributable to the new controller.\n\n---\n\n## Context \u2014 what's broken\n\nA 2-hour rollout window on 2026-05-13 observed **73 GPU workers spawned, 70 killed before becoming productive, 1 worker did all the actual work** (4% effective utilisation). The same pattern is present in git history going back at least 6 weeks.\n\nTwo parallel Codex GPT-5.5 sense-checks (independent runs, no cross-talk) reading `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` converged on a single architectural diagnosis, **subsequently verified by a third Codex `high` review** against the actual code:\n\n> The orchestrator runs **two independent capacity controllers** in one cycle. `_handle_early_termination` (around `control_loop.py:340`, `current_capacity` derived at `:362`, gate check at `:377`) computes one `desired` figure from `task_counts.total * scale_down_multiplier` + min GPUs + idle buffer. Later in the same cycle, `_calculate_scaling_decision` calls `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` at `control_loop.py:802` (when `use_new_scaling_logic` is enabled), which emits spawn action math from queued coverage (`workers_covering_tasks = idle + spawning`, `uncovered_tasks = queued - workers_covering_tasks`, `spawn_for_tasks = uncovered_tasks`, see `worker_state.py:588`) plus a busy-worker buffer floor (`worker_state.py:594`). **However**, `desired_workers` itself is still populated from the legacy `total_workload * scale_up_multiplier`, `busy + idle buffer`, and `min_active_gpus` block at `worker_state.py:616`.\n>\n> The bug is **not** \"stale snapshot between phases\" \u2014 the scaling decision is computed *after* early-term, not before. The bug is that early-term **mutates the worker list** (terminating spawning workers) before a second, separate scaling formula runs against the same phase-1 task snapshot and the now-changed worker list. Two formulas, two notions of `desired`, one cycle, opposing actions.\n\nThree named failure modes ride on this defect:\n\n- **Spawn churn from snapshot gates.** `_handle_early_termination` is guarded by `if task_counts.queued > 0` (at `control_loop.py:377`). `task_counts.queued` is an instantaneous snapshot taken once at the top of each cycle. When the single productive worker had just claimed a segment (queue momentarily empty, six in-progress segments active), early-term fired and killed the spawning replacement. Next cycle re-spawned. Loop.\n- **No backoff for RunPod `spawn_failed`.** 55 of the 73 deaths were RunPod refusing pod allocation. The orchestrator retried at 30-second cycle cadence indefinitely.\n- **Mutating actions sequenced before mismatched re-derivation.** Early-term terminates spawning workers from the worker list, then `_calculate_scaling_decision` re-derives `desired` against the same phase-1 task snapshot but a now-shorter worker list, and decides to re-spawn what early-term just killed.\n\nA team-authored postmortem (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-postmortem-20260513.md`) and approach memo (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-approach-20260513.md`) cover the symptom-level analysis. The architectural prescription below is the *deep* fix.\n\n### Duplicate code path \u2014 clarified\n\n`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` is **the** production runtime path. `main.py` imports `OrchestratorControlLoop` from it. The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree contains duplicated chunks of capacity logic (including a duplicate `_handle_early_termination`) but is **only exercised by tests, not by production**. Default deletion target for this sprint: the mixin duplication under `control/phases/`. The planner may override that choice only by first showing concretely that the mixins contain logic worth migrating into `control_loop.py` (or by proposing to flip production over to the mixin path \u2014 but that is out of scope for this sprint and would be a separate brief).\n\n---\n\n## Architecture \u2014 what we're building\n\n### New Postgres table\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE public.worker_capacity_intents (\n  id                          uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n  pool                        text NOT NULL,\n  route_key                   text,                              -- nullable for pool-wide intents\n  desired_capacity            int  NOT NULL,\n  reason                      text NOT NULL,                     -- 'queued_coverage'|'min_floor'|'cooldown'|...\n  observed_queued             int,\n  observed_active             int,\n  observed_spawning           int,\n  cycle_id                    bigint,\n  created_at                  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  valid_until                 timestamptz,                       -- intent expires; absence \u2260 kill\n  stable_since                timestamptz,                       -- when desired_capacity last changed\n  consecutive_spawn_failures  int  NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\n  next_spawn_allowed_at       timestamptz,                       -- backoff cooldown\n  shadow                      boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true      -- shadow vs authoritative mode\n);\n\nCREATE INDEX worker_capacity_intents_pool_route_created\n  ON public.worker_capacity_intents (pool, route_key, created_at DESC);\n```\n\n**One row written per cycle per pool**, regardless of whether the intent changed. This is the audit trail.\n\n**Migration location:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/` (alongside the existing `2025*_*.sql` files). Filename pattern: `20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`. The repo's existing migration registration system applies \u2014 planner should locate how it's wired (likely `scripts/` or a runner referenced from Railway config) and follow the established pattern.\n\n### Logging \u2014 `system_logs` constraint\n\nExisting `system_logs.valid_source_type` CHECK constraint permits only `orchestrator_gpu`, `orchestrator_api`, and `worker`. **Do not invent a new `source_type` value.** Use `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` with a metadata tag field (e.g., `metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' = 'true'`) to distinguish shadow log entries from authoritative ones. Saves a migration and avoids breaking other readers of `system_logs`.\n\n### New Python module \u2014 `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`\n\nOwns the full five-phase state machine. Pure-where-possible, side-effects concentrated at boundaries.\n\n```python\nclass CapacityReconciler:\n    def reconcile(self, cycle_id: int) -> ReconciliationResult:\n        observed = self.observe()                     # phase 1: read workers + tasks\n        plan = self.plan(observed)                    # phase 2: compute CapacityPlan\n        admitted = self.admit(plan)                   # phase 3: filter by backoff/cooldown\n        result = self.act(admitted, shadow=self.shadow_mode)  # phase 4: side effects (or log-only)\n        self.record(cycle_id, observed, plan, admitted, result)  # phase 5: persist intent row\n        return result\n```\n\nWhere:\n\n- **`observe()`** is the canonical read of `workers` + `tasks` state. Counts active / spawning / route-stale / idle, computes `total_tasks = queued + in_progress`. Returns a frozen `Observed` dataclass \u2014 **immutable**, taken at one moment in time, passed by value through subsequent phases.\n- **`plan(observed)`** computes ONE `desired_capacity` from ONE formula. Returns a `CapacityPlan` with `desired`, `effective_capacity = active + spawning - admitted_pending_cancels`, `actions = [Spawn | CancelPendingSpawn | TerminateIdle | Noop]`, `reason`, `valid_until`.\n- **`admit(plan)`** is admission control. Checks `next_spawn_allowed_at` per pool, drops `Spawn` actions during cooldown, logs `suppressed_by_backoff`.\n- **`act(admitted, shadow)`** executes side effects when `shadow=False`. When `shadow=True`, logs intended actions to `system_logs` (source_type=`orchestrator_gpu`, metadata tag `shadow_intended_action`). **In shadow mode, `act()` MUST NOT call RunPod, MUST NOT mutate worker rows, MUST NOT update backoff state. Shadow is read-only on the external world.**\n- **`record(...)`** writes one row to `worker_capacity_intents` regardless of mode. The `shadow` boolean column distinguishes shadow rows from authoritative rows.\n\n### Rules the reconciler MUST honor (invariants)\n\nThese are invariants, not preferences. The cycle-trace simulation tests in the deliverables section exist to enforce them.\n\n1. **Spawning workers count as capacity** until they (a) report `spawn_failed`, (b) exceed `active_initializing_max_sec`, or (c) are explicitly cancelled by a `CancelPendingSpawn` action emitted by this reconciler.\n2. **Never kill or cancel a spawning worker because `queued == 0`.** The queue snapshot does not have authority over an authorized spawn.\n3. **Cancel `Spawn` action emission** only when the desired_capacity has remained \u2264 effective for **at least two consecutive observed reconciler snapshots spanning at least `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time**, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Wall-clock based, not iteration-count based \u2014 duplicate Railway replicas or delayed cycles make raw cycle count unreliable.\n4. **Scale down active workers** only via idle timeout + min/buffer constraints. Active workers are never killed by capacity-pressure logic.\n5. **RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff is keyed by `(pool, route_key)`**, not globally. `consecutive_spawn_failures += 1` and `next_spawn_allowed_at = now() + min(2^N * 30s, 600s)`. Successful spawn for that `(pool, route_key)` resets the counter to 0.\n6. **`record()` writes exactly one row per pool per cycle**, even when nothing changed. Audit trail integrity.\n7. **No authoritative spawn action executes without a prior `RECORD` write succeeding** within the same reconciliation. This means `act(shadow=False)` calls `record()` first and only then dispatches RunPod creates / cancellations. Idempotency: if `act()` partially completes and the process crashes, the next cycle sees the recorded intent and can resume.\n8. **Single-writer per pool per cycle in authoritative mode.** Use a Postgres advisory lock keyed on `hashtext(pool)` or a lease row in a small `orchestrator_leases` table, held for the duration of `admit()` + `act()`. Without this, two Railway replicas can each spawn against the same pool's intent and double-provision. (Shadow mode does not need the lock \u2014 multiple shadows are fine since they don't mutate.)\n9. **At cutover, the reconciler must adopt pre-existing spawning workers** that have no associated intent row. These were spawned by the legacy controller before cutover. Treat them as part of effective_capacity; assign a synthetic adoption intent on first observe, do not cancel them.\n\n### Phasing \u2014 what gets deleted, what survives\n\n**Deleted:**\n- `_handle_early_termination` (the entire phase 2 block in `control_loop.py` at lines ~340-400).\n- The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree (test-only duplicate code path). Includes any tests that exercised it; replace those with tests against the new reconciler.\n\n**Survives:**\n- Worker lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers`, heartbeat checks, promote-to-active logic) \u2014 stays in `control_loop.py`.\n- RunPod pod creation primitives (`runpod_client.create_pod` and friends) \u2014 stay. `act()` calls into them.\n- `min_active_gpus`, `machines_to_keep_idle`, `buffer_based` calculations \u2014 stay as **inputs** to `plan()`. **Out of scope for this sprint** is simplifying or renaming them; `plan()` consumes them and emits one authoritative `desired` from one formula. The planner MUST write down exactly that composition (what's the precedence order between `min_active_gpus` floor, `queued_coverage`, and `busy_buffer`?) in the plan output, and gate must verify it's specified.\n\n---\n\n## Rollout strategy \u2014 shadow mode then cutover\n\nThis is a production control loop with money burn and destructive actions. Cutover happens behind a feature flag, gated on observed divergence behavior.\n\n### Phase A \u2014 Shadow deployment (cycle ordering matters)\n\n- Reconciler ships with `shadow_mode=True` (env var: `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=shadow|authoritative`).\n- **Cycle ordering \u2014 explicit, not implicit:** at the start of each orchestrator cycle, after lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers` etc.) but **before** the legacy `_handle_early_termination` and `_calculate_scaling_decision`, capture an immutable `Observed` snapshot. Pass that frozen snapshot to the shadow reconciler. Let the legacy controller proceed with its normal logic on its own snapshot. Compare outcomes afterward.\n\n  This ordering is non-negotiable: if the shadow runs after legacy mutations, divergence data is contaminated by what legacy already did, and the audit becomes meaningless.\n\n- Shadow does `OBSERVE \u2192 PLAN \u2192 ADMIT \u2192 RECORD`. **It does NOT call `act()` in shadow mode.** Intent rows are written with `shadow=true`.\n- Existing two-controller path continues to be authoritative. Nothing user-facing changes.\n- A divergence audit query becomes the validation surface:\n  ```sql\n  -- Shadow's intended actions per cycle vs. legacy's actual outcomes per cycle, last 24h\n  SELECT\n    sl.created_at::date,\n    sl.metadata->>'pool' AS pool,\n    sl.metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' AS shadow_action,\n    -- join against actual worker-row mutations from legacy controller\n    ...\n  FROM system_logs sl\n  WHERE sl.source_type = 'orchestrator_gpu'\n    AND sl.metadata ? 'shadow_intended_action'\n    AND sl.created_at > now() - interval '24 hours';\n  ```\n  (Planner should write the full join against the `workers` table mutations or against a parallel `legacy_actions` log if one exists.)\n\n### Phase B \u2014 Cutover decision criteria\n\nCutover from shadow to authoritative requires ALL of:\n\n1. **\u226524 hours of shadow mode in production traffic AND \u2265N meaningful capacity events.** \"Meaningful event\" = a cycle where shadow_desired \u2260 legacy_action OR shadow proposed `CancelPendingSpawn` OR backoff suppressed a spawn. N = at least 50; if 24h of normal traffic does not produce 50 such events, extend shadow window until they accumulate.\n2. **Replay validation against captured production state.** Before cutover, the planner must produce a replay test that loads a captured snapshot of `workers` + `tasks` from the actual 2026-05-13 incident window (or a similar pathological window) and runs the reconciler against it. Assert: at the moment `queued` momentarily drops to 0 with active work in flight, the reconciler does NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn` for the spawning worker. This is the **non-negotiable proof of correctness**.\n3. **No divergence in scenarios where the legacy controller's behavior is correct.** If shadow says spawn while legacy correctly says terminate-idle on a genuinely-idle worker, that's a reconciler bug, not a legacy bug \u2014 fix and re-shadow.\n\n### Phase C \u2014 Cutover\n\n- Flip `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=authoritative`. Reconciler becomes authoritative; legacy controller becomes a no-op stub kept for one more day.\n- **At the moment of flag flip**, the reconciler's first OBSERVE will discover spawning workers with no prior intent row (they were spawned by legacy). Apply rule 9 above (synthetic adoption intent).\n- Watch `scaling-audit --hours 6` output for 4 hours post-cutover. Effective utilisation should rise from baseline.\n- 24h later: delete legacy stub, delete shadow-mode logging, delete the duplicate mixin code path.\n\n---\n\n## Deliverables (acceptance criteria)\n\nThe plan must produce, and execute must ship:\n\n1. **Migration:** `worker_capacity_intents` table + indexes in `sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`, registered with the orchestrator's existing migration system.\n2. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`** + supporting modules (`capacity_plan.py` for pure PLAN computation, types/dataclasses for `CapacityPlan` / `Action` / `Observed`).\n3. **`_handle_early_termination` deleted** from `control_loop.py`. Verified by grep returning zero matches.\n4. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree deleted** (and any tests that exercised it removed or rewritten against the new reconciler).\n5. **Single-writer lease/lock mechanism** for authoritative mode (advisory lock or `orchestrator_leases` table; planner picks, defends choice in plan output).\n6. **Cycle-trace simulation tests** under `tests/control/` covering:\n   - **Test A \u2014 Queued snapshot gate:** Queued drops to zero mid-render while 1 active worker has 5 in-progress segments and a spawning replacement is in flight. Reconciler must NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn`. Spawning worker survives.\n   - **Test B \u2014 Spawn backoff with fake clock:** Use a fake/frozen clock. RunPod returns `spawn_failed` 4 times in succession. Assert exact `next_spawn_allowed_at` after each failure (30s, 60s, 120s, 240s). After the clock advances past `next_spawn_allowed_at`, a fifth attempt succeeds and `consecutive_spawn_failures` resets to 0. **Required: a `Clock` injection seam** in the reconciler \u2014 no `datetime.now()` called directly inside `plan()` or `admit()`.\n   - **Test C \u2014 Time-based hysteresis on cancel:** Spawning worker has been spawning for 250s when `desired_capacity` drops from 2 to 1. Reconciler must NOT immediately cancel. Cancellation may only emit when desired \u2264 effective has held for \u2265 `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time across at least two snapshots, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Test both branches.\n   - **Test D \u2014 Route-stale workers:** A worker with `is_route_stale=True` (per `worker_state.py:83` definition based on `worker_contract_version` and route contract compatibility) is present in the worker list. Reconciler must NOT count it toward `effective_capacity` when satisfying a fresh-route `desired`.\n   - **Test E \u2014 Cutover adoption:** Pre-existing spawning worker rows with no intent-table predecessor are correctly adopted on first authoritative `observe()`. They are not cancelled. A synthetic adoption intent row is written.\n   - **Test F \u2014 Replay against captured incident state:** Load fixture state representing the 2026-05-13 14:11-14:14 window (or equivalent synthetic pathological state). Run reconciler. Assert no oscillation, no premature cancel.\n7. **Shadow-mode infrastructure:** `shadow_mode` flag, immutable `Observed` snapshot captured at the correct point in the cycle (before legacy capacity actions), shadow logging via `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` + metadata tag, divergence audit SQL committed to `scripts/` or `sql/`.\n8. **`scaling-audit` debug command extended** in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` to read `worker_capacity_intents` and report on shadow vs. authoritative cycles, divergence counts, and per-pool intent stability. Cross-repo touch; small and well-scoped.\n9. **Feature-flag cutover plumbing:** `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE` env var controls shadow vs. authoritative. No-op stub of legacy controller for 24h post-cutover.\n\n---\n\n## Out of scope (do not touch in this sprint)\n\n- **Cross-pool intent reasoning.** Each pool reconciled independently. Cross-pool rebalancing is a separate brief.\n- **Simplifying `min_active_gpus` / `machines_to_keep_idle` / `buffer_based`.** Stays as input to `plan()`. The plan output must *specify* the composition (precedence order), but not change the input semantics.\n- **UI / dashboard for the intent table.** `SELECT` queries are the interface.\n- **Worker-side code (`reigh-worker/`).** Untouched.\n- **App-side code (`reigh-app/`).** Only the cross-repo touch is extending `scaling-audit` debug command, which is small and well-scoped.\n- **Replacing the existing config/feature-flag system.** Use whatever's already there.\n- **Flipping production to the `control/phases/` mixin path.** Out of scope. The mixins are being deleted, not promoted.\n\n---\n\n## Watch items (gate signals)\n\nThe gate phase MUST verify each of these before allowing execute to proceed:\n\n- **W1: Concurrency on `worker_capacity_intents` writes.** Multiple orchestrator replicas could exist (Railway autoscaling). Planner must specify uniqueness strategy: `UNIQUE (pool, cycle_id)` constraint, advisory lock around the write, or accept duplicates and let queries dedupe. Gate verifies the choice is defensible.\n- **W2: Cutover sequencing.** What happens to in-flight spawning workers at the moment of `shadow_mode=False` flip? Rule 9 specifies adoption; gate verifies the implementation actually does it.\n- **W3: Duplicate code path deletion completeness.** Verify by grep that `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` is removed AND no production code path imports from it AND any test rewrites are passing.\n- **W4: Idempotency under cycle restart.** If `act()` partially completes (spawned one of two pods, then process crashes), the next cycle must not double-spawn. Rule 7 addresses this; gate verifies the implementation.\n- **W5: Test fidelity.** Test F's captured-state fixture must be realistic. Planner should examine actual `workers` + `tasks` snapshots from the incident window if available, or synthetic state that reproduces the failure mode.\n- **W6: Single-writer semantics.** Verify the advisory lock or lease implementation prevents two Railway replicas from acting on the same pool's intent simultaneously. Test by spawning two reconciler instances in the test harness against the same DB.\n- **W7: Migration registration.** Verify the new migration in `sql/` is wired into whatever runner the orchestrator uses (likely a script invoked at Railway deploy time or referenced from `railway.json`). Migration must be idempotent.\n- **W8: Cutover adoption test passes.** Test E above must pass before any cutover plan is signed off. Pre-existing spawning workers must not be orphaned or cancelled.\n\n---\n\n## Risk: what would falsify the architectural premise\n\nIf during prep or plan the planner discovers that the \"two formulas computing different `desired`\" diagnosis is wrong \u2014 e.g., the early-term and scale-up phases actually coordinate correctly through some mechanism this brief overlooked \u2014 **stop and report**. Do not proceed with deleting `_handle_early_termination` based on a faulty premise.\n\nLess drastically: if the planner finds that the `CapacityPlan` abstraction can't cleanly express some real-world scenario in the existing `worker_state.py` decision logic (around lines 588-780), surface that during critique. The right answer might be a different decomposition than OBSERVE/PLAN/ADMIT/ACT/RECORD.\n\nThe whole point of running this at `high` author depth is so the planner can push back on the brief's design if it's wrong.\n\n---\n\n## Reference materials in the repo\n\n- `docs/scaling-churn-postmortem-20260513.md` \u2014 symptom-level postmortem.\n- `docs/scaling-churn-approach-20260513.md` \u2014 approach memo that this brief is the formal version of.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` \u2014 file being substantially rewritten. Key lines: `:340` (early-term entry), `:362` (current_capacity derivation), `:377` (queued > 0 gate), `:802` (scaling decision call).\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py` \u2014 `:83` (`is_route_stale` definition), `:588` (queued coverage spawn math), `:594` (busy-worker buffer floor), `:616-780` (legacy `desired_workers` block and existing decision logic).\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` \u2014 test-only mixin tree being deleted.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/` \u2014 migration directory; existing files use `YYYYMMDDhhmmss_descriptive_name.sql` pattern.\n- `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` \u2014 `scaling-audit` command lives here; extend to read the new table.\n- `reigh-worker-orchestrator/railway.json` \u2014 Railway deploy config; check for migration runner wiring.\n\nPrevious successful rollout for reference pattern: `brief-stop-the-cross-boundary-20260513-1358` (today, merged to main as commits `b1675676c`, `04fd9d759`, `d813238a3` in reigh-app; `3bfe7ac9` in reigh-worker; `fcec14f` in reigh-worker-orchestrator).",

      {
        "id": "FLAG-001",
        "concern": "Single-writer: pg_try_advisory_xact_lock cannot work as written against PostgREST/Supabase. Advisory locks are transaction-scoped, but each supabase.rpc() / supabase.table().execute() call is its own HTTP request and its own transaction. Two Railway replicas will acquire+release the lock between independent statements and proceed in lockstep. Plan must specify either (a) an RPC function that owns the full admit+act transaction, or (b) a leases-table mechanism with conditional UPDATE inside an RPC.",
        "resolution": "v2 addresses 20 significant flags from the gate, clustered on five concrete defects. (1) Single-writer: replaced PostgREST-incompatible advisory locks with a Postgres RPC `func_reconcile_capacity` that owns lease + state-mutation + intent-INSERT in one transaction, backed by a new `orchestrator_leases` table. (2) Storage split: `worker_capacity_intents` stays append-only audit with `cycle_uuid` (no `UNIQUE(pool, cycle_id)`); mutable backoff moves into a new `worker_capacity_state` table keyed on `(pool, route_key)`. (3) Backoff trigger: `consecutive_spawn_failures` is now derived in OBSERVE from the `workers` table over a configurable lookback window, covering all spawning-phase termination reasons (`gpu_not_detected`, `ready_not_claiming_timeout`, etc.), not just synchronous `spawn_failed` returns; same derivation in shadow and authoritative so suppression events are observable pre-cutover. (4) Spawn primitive: extracted `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` into a free `spawn_one_worker` function; the reconciler calls this same primitive (no `runpod.create_pod` invention). (5) Legacy `desired` removal: `_execute_scaling`'s idle-timeout sizing block now consumes `self._last_reconciliation.plan.desired` in authoritative mode, so the legacy formula stops being authoritative anywhere. Also added: Railway `releaseCommand` for migration application, direct-INSERT path for shadow logs (bypasses logger that nests extras under `metadata.extra`), real-incident-fixture capture script (synthetic only as documented fallback), audit of `setup_database.py`/`show_migrations.py` for migration enumeration, new Tests G/H/I/J for single-writer/idempotency/audit-key/lifecycle-backoff. Cross-repo `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` touch demoted to a separate ticket; in-repo `scripts/scaling_audit_cli.py` replaces it for Phase B validation."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-002",
        "concern": "UNIQUE (pool, cycle_id) collides on process restart and prevents post-spawn-failure backoff updates. OrchestratorControlLoop.cycle_count resets to 0 in __init__ (control_loop.py:52); Step 6 implements update_intent_backoff as another INSERT into the same table, which violates the unique constraint. Needs monotonic/UUID cycle key, a sub-cycle sequence column, or backoff state in a separate table.",
        "resolution": "v2 addresses 20 significant flags from the gate, clustered on five concrete defects. (1) Single-writer: replaced PostgREST-incompatible advisory locks with a Postgres RPC `func_reconcile_capacity` that owns lease + state-mutation + intent-INSERT in one transaction, backed by a new `orchestrator_leases` table. (2) Storage split: `worker_capacity_intents` stays append-only audit with `cycle_uuid` (no `UNIQUE(pool, cycle_id)`); mutable backoff moves into a new `worker_capacity_state` table keyed on `(pool, route_key)`. (3) Backoff trigger: `consecutive_spawn_failures` is now derived in OBSERVE from the `workers` table over a configurable lookback window, covering all spawning-phase termination reasons (`gpu_not_detected`, `ready_not_claiming_timeout`, etc.), not just synchronous `spawn_failed` returns; same derivation in shadow and authoritative so suppression events are observable pre-cutover. (4) Spawn primitive: extracted `OrchestratorControlLoop._spawn_worker` into a free `spawn_one_worker` function; the reconciler calls this same primitive (no `runpod.create_pod` invention). (5) Legacy `desired` removal: `_execute_scaling`'s idle-timeout sizing block now consumes `self._last_reconciliation.plan.desired` in authoritative mode, so the legacy formula stops being authoritative anywhere. Also added: Railway `releaseCommand` for migration application, direct-INSERT path for shadow logs (bypasses logger that nests extras under `metadata.extra`), real-incident-fixture capture script (synthetic only as documented fallback), audit of `setup_database.py`/`show_migrations.py` for migration enumeration, new Tests G/H/I/J for single-writer/idempotency/audit-key/lifecycle-backoff. Cross-repo `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` touch demoted to a separate ticket; in-repo `scripts/scaling_audit_cli.py` replaces it for Phase B validation."

      {
        "id": "FLAG-010",
        "concern": "Cross-repo scaling-audit extension (reigh-app/scripts/debug.py) is deferred without acceptance-test detail. Acceptance criterion #14 depends on it; executor in this repo likely cannot reach the cross-repo, risking a half-shipped deliverable.",
        "resolution": "Plan Step 11; brief deliverables item 8; success criterion priority 'should'."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-011",
        "concern": "Migration deploy plumbing remains non-functional even with the new releaseCommand. scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py invokes supabase.rpc('sql', {'query': statement}) \u2014 that RPC is not part of Supabase/PostgREST's standard API; the script's own fallback at lines 76-79 prints 'needs manual execution' on failure and continues to a 'completed!' banner with exit 0. show_migrations.py is pure display. setup_database.py references SQL files that don't exist in the current sql/ directory. Adding a releaseCommand around any of these will succeed silently while leaving the new tables/RPC uncreated in production Postgres. Plan needs a real deploy mechanism: an out-of-band psql via the Supabase pooler connection string, a one-shot job using a CLI like supabase-cli's `supabase db push`, or rewriting apply_sql_migrations.py to use a working DDL channel (e.g., PG connection via the Supabase pooler URL, not PostgREST).",
        "resolution": "v3 closes nine concrete gaps the gate surfaced in v2. (1) Migration deploy actually works: rewrites scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py to use psycopg over the Supabase pooler DATABASE_URL with a _schema_migrations ledger, auto-discovers sql/*.sql, and exits non-zero on failure. Changes gpu_orchestrator/railway.json build to dockerContext='.', dockerfilePath='gpu_orchestrator/Dockerfile' so scripts/ and sql/ land in the image, updates the Dockerfile's COPY directives accordingly, and adds releaseCommand='python -m scripts.apply_sql_migrations'. (2) Lease acquisition is now an internal IF/ELSIF inside the PL/pgSQL RPC that always returns a stable jsonb {lease_acquired: bool, ...} \u2014 no caller-side row-count interpretation. (3) Lease TTL = 3 * orchestrator_poll_sec (default 90 s), exposed as OrchestratorConfig.lease_ttl_seconds. (4) Backoff source-of-truth: workers table is canonical, worker_capacity_state holds only the cache fields (next_spawn_allowed_at, last_spawn_failure_*); consecutive_spawn_failures is recomputed every cycle from OBSERVE. (5) Backoff reset condition: explicitly documented as a deliberate deviation from brief invariant 5 \u2014 reset on promotion-to-active (lifecycle ACTIVE_*), not on pod creation, because pod creation can degrade into gpu_not_detected / ready_not_claiming_timeout. (6) New Test K proves cache invalidation under simulated orchestrator restart. (7) RLS pattern mirrors sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql:259-261 \u2014 plain GRANT to authenticated/anon/service_role, no RLS, matching what the rest of sql/ does. (8) intent_row_id is documented as additive in the spawn metadata dict; explicit grep confirms no collision with existing keys (runpod_id, termination_reason, ssh_details, pod_details, ram_tier, storage_volume, selected_route metadata). (9) Reconciler accepts the CycleSummary reference and increments workers_spawned / workers_terminated for its own actions (new Test L). Also added Test M (migration runner round-trip against ephemeral Postgres) so the new deploy mechanism is exercised in CI."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-012",

        "id": "FLAG-013",
        "concern": "Backoff source-of-truth ambiguity. Step 5.1 derives BackoffState in OBSERVE from workers history each cycle; Step 1.2 and the RPC description treat worker_capacity_state as the durable backoff store, upserted by the RPC. Plan does not say which is canonical when they disagree (e.g., after a crash where workers shows N failures but worker_capacity_state was last updated at N-2). Specify which wins (likely OBSERVE-derived, with worker_capacity_state as a cache for next_spawn_allowed_at across cycles) and add a test for the divergence case.",
        "resolution": "Step 5.1 (derive in OBSERVE) + Step 1.2 (mutable table) + Step 1.4 (RPC upserts). No reconciliation rule between them."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-014",
        "concern": "Lease TTL is not specified. orchestrator_leases.expires_at must have a chosen value; too short (~poll_sec) flaps the lease during a slow cycle; too long delays recovery on a crashed replica.         "resolution": "Step 1.3 (orchestrator_leases schema) + Step 1.4 (acquire/refresh) do not pick a TTL constant; gpu_orchestrator/railway.json shows numReplicas: 1 today."

        "id": "FLAG-015",
        "concern": "Reset condition deviates from brief. Brief invariant 5: 'Successful spawn for that (pool, route_key) resets the counter to 0.'         "resolution": "Brief invariant 5; plan Step 5.1."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-016",
        "concern": "ON CONFLICT lease-acquisition RETURNING semantics. When the existing lease row's WHERE filter (expires_at < now() OR holder_id = caller) evaluates false, Postgres performs no UPDATE and RETURNING yields zero rows \u2014 not 'lease_acquired = false'. An executor reading 'INSERT ... RETURNING holder_id = $caller' literally may treat the empty result as falsy in some clients and truthy in others (driver-dependent). Plan should call out the explicit 'rows returned == 0 \u2192 not acquired' contract and assert it in Test G.",
        "resolution": "Step 1.4 lease-acquisition SQL sketch."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-017",
        "concern": "CycleSummary integration missing. Plan's reconciler act() updates DB rows but doesn't mention updating CycleSummary. Post-cutover, those counters under-report and any alerting / observability built on them goes silent.",
        "resolution": "control_loop.py:421 (summary.workers_terminated += 1 in _handle_early_termination); control_loop.py:1038 (summary.workers_spawned += 1 in _execute_scaling); control_loop.py:150-153 (health_monitor consumes these). Plan Steps 5/7 don't address."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-018",
        "concern": "First-deploy ledger bootstrap missing. The rewritten apply_sql_migrations.py auto-discovers ALL sql/*.sql files and tracks them in _schema_migrations(filename pk). Most files use IF NOT EXISTS / CREATE OR REPLACE / DO $$ guarded ALTERs and are idempotent, but sql/20250121000001_fix_heartbeat_default.sql contains a non-idempotent UPDATE that clears `last_heartbeat` for any worker whose heartbeat lands within 5 seconds of created_at \u2014 a real possibility during deploy bursts. Plan should pre-populate the ledger with the six already-applied migrations (or add a one-time SQL bootstrap step), so first deploy doesn't replay legacy DML.",
        "resolution": "v4 closes the seven remaining open flags from v3's gate review without changing the architecture. (1) FLAG-018 / issue_hints-1 \u2014 the rewritten migration runner now pre-populates `_schema_migrations` with the six legacy filenames when it detects an established DB (proxy: `public.system_logs` table exists), so the first production releaseCommand does NOT replay `sql/20250121000001_fix_heartbeat_default.sql`'s non-idempotent `UPDATE workers SET last_heartbeat = NULL ...`. On a fresh dev DB without `system_logs`, all migrations apply in order as normal. (2) issue_hints-2 / all_locations-1 / all_locations-2 \u2014 added a Phase 0 'Operator pre-flight' section reproducing the exact checklist that must appear in the PR description: `SUPABASE_DB_URL` set on Railway, prod-Dockerfile diff'd against this PR, Railway dockerContext honoured (JSON or UI/.railwayignore fallback), DB role has CREATE FUNCTION + GRANT, staging deploy successful. PR is not mergeable with any unchecked box. (3) correctness-1 \u2014 explicitly specified the `postgres`-role pooler URL (not service_role), and the runner now executes `SELECT current_user` at startup and exits non-zero if the role is `service_role` or `anon`. (4) correctness-2 \u2014 the runner regex-rejects files containing top-level `BEGIN;` / `COMMIT;` / `ROLLBACK;` to keep the per-file transaction contract clean (`DO $$ ... BEGIN ... END $$` blocks remain fine). (5) scope \u2014 added a CODEOWNERS entry for `sql/` and a PR-template checklist item, so future SQL drops are reviewed before auto-apply. (6) callers (new in v3 gate) \u2014 `reconciler_cancel_pending_spawn` is excluded from the spawning-failure reason set with an explicit code comment explaining the rationale (authorized cancel, not a failure, would create a feedback loop). Test J extended to assert this. (7) Test M scenarios expanded: established-DB bootstrap, fresh-DB full apply, BEGIN/COMMIT rejection, role check, all in one CI fixture. Architecture, DECISION-001 through DECISION-014, single-writer RPC, storage split, backoff source-of-truth, spawn primitive refactor, _execute_scaling fed by reconciler desired, shadow direct-INSERT \u2014 all unchanged from v3."
      },

        "id": "FLAG-021",
        "concern": "Migration runner DB role not specified.         "resolution": "Plan Step 2.1 mentions SUPABASE_DB_URL / DATABASE_URL generically; sql/20260514000003_func_reconcile_capacity.sql will need CREATE FUNCTION; sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql will need GRANT."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-022",
        "concern": "psycopg multi-statement contract is implicit.         "resolution": "Plan Step 2.1 'execute the file's contents as a single statement block'; sql/ today has no files violating the rule."

      "id": "correctness-4",
      "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Backoff reset condition: invariant 5 says 'Successful spawn for that (pool, route_key) resets the counter to 0.' Plan Step 5.1 defines consecutive_spawn_failures as 'count of consecutive failures since the most recent successful promotion-to-active for (pool, route_key).' That's a different signal \u2014 a worker's spawn-attempt succeeds when the pod is created (current spawn_failed surface), but 'promotion to active' is the lifecycle completion. These are minutes apart. The brief's reset condition wants reset-on-successful-pod-creation; the plan's wants reset-on-promotion-to-active, which delays reset and overcounts failures during the initialization window. Probably the planner's choice is better (more conservative), but the deviation from the brief should be acknowledged.",

This widens the implicit contract: any future SQL file dropped into sql/ will be auto-applied at next deploy, including files added by other PRs or merge conflicts that an operator might miss. Existing manually-driven workflow (Supabase SQL Editor copy-paste) had a human gatekeeper. The plan does not propose a review checklist or PR-template gate around new sql/ files.     "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Backoff reset condition: invariant 5 says 'Successful spawn for that (pool, route_key) resets the counter to 0.' Plan Step 5.1 defines consecutive_spawn_failures as 'count of consecutive failures since the most recent successful promotion-to-active for (pool, route_key).' That's a different signal \u2014 a worker's spawn-attempt succeeds when the pod is created (current spawn_failed surface), but 'promotion to active' is the lifecycle completion. These are minutes apart. The brief's reset condition wants reset-on-successful-pod-creation; the plan's wants reset-on-promotion-to-active, which delays reset and overcounts failures during the initialization window. Probably the planner's choice is better (more conservative), but the deviation from the brief should be acknowledged.",

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
